## feat(ratings): MCP rating tools and Platform Operator read access (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/conversation-ratings`) — PR 4 of [`planning/conversation-rating-plan.md`](../../planning/conversation-rating-plan.md)

### What changed and why

Ratings can now be read and triaged from an MCP client, and the Platform Operator can answer questions about them.

- **MCP** — [`McpRatingTools`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpRatingTools.java): `list_conversation_ratings` (every `/ratings` filter incl. `needsAttention`, `reviewStatus`, `conversationId`; `sort`, `index`, `limit`), `summarize_conversation_ratings` (`groupBy`), `count_ratings_needing_attention` and `review_conversation_rating` (REVIEWED / NEW and a note). Registered in `McpToolFilter`; 89 → 93 tools, 292 arguments of which 128 are required.
- **Operator** — [`tool-scopes.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/operator/tool-scopes.ts) allow-lists `GET /ratings`, `GET /ratings/summary` and `GET /ratings/attention-count`; [`system-prompt.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/operator/system-prompt.ts) gains a ratings section (what a rating holds, `ratingConfig` and its defaults, needs-attention, `AGENT_ID_REQUIRED`, how to answer "how is agent X rated since version N"), gated on a new `grantsRatingReads` predicate. Operator revision 39 (37 when written; main had meanwhile claimed 37 too, so the merge made it 38, and clarifying the date-only `to` in the prompt made it 39).
- **Docs** — [MCP Server](../mcp-server.md#rating-tools-4) (section, count, Author tier row), [Conversation Ratings](../conversation-ratings.md#mcp-tools-and-the-platform-operator), and a "Feedback from users" section in the `eddi-admin` Claude Code skill.

### Design decisions

- **Access is the REST rule, run for the MCP caller.** The tools inject the in-process `IRestConversationRatingAdmin` bean — the pattern `McpAdminTools` uses with `IRestAgentAdministration` — not the loopback REST client, which sends no credentials (401 with authorization on, no scoping with it off). That bean's `ResourceAccessGuard` and `ConversationAccessGuard` read the request's `SecurityIdentity`, i.e. the MCP caller: admin sees everything, an editor names an agent it holds `EDIT` on, `userId` for admins only, `canOpenTranscript` as on REST. Nothing is re-implemented, so MCP cannot drift from the Feedback page.
- **Tier `McpRoles.AUTHOR`** (admin, editor) — equal to the REST `@RolesAllowed`, pinned by `McpRoleTierParityTest` (`IRestConversationRatingAdmin` added to the mirrors). `eddi-viewer` stays out.
- **Reviewer = the caller's plain principal** (`callerActor`), as on REST — not the HITL tools' `mcp:<principal>` form — so one person is one reviewer whichever surface they triage from.
- **Errors keep the REST codes** as `errorCode` (`AGENT_ID_REQUIRED`, `RATING_CHANGED`, `INVALID_PARAMETER`, …). A guard refusal (`io.quarkus.security.ForbiddenException`) is mapped to `FORBIDDEN` explicitly: left to `toolFailure` it is not "caller-facing" and would surface as `INTERNAL`.
- **No writes for the operator.** `PATCH /ratings/{ratingId}` and `POST /ratings/review` record the caller as the person who looked at the feedback, and a bulk review empties the queue in one call — that judgement stays with a human on the Feedback page. `GET /ratings/export` is a `text/csv` download of up to 100,000 rows (userIds included for an admin) that no chat answer needs. Both are documented as deliberate exclusions in the allow-list.
- MCP export and bulk review are left out too; both stay REST and Manager only.

### Tests

- `McpRatingToolsTest` (13) wires the real `RestConversationRatingAdmin` with mocked guards and store: role tier, `AGENT_ID_REQUIRED`, missing `EDIT`, userId redaction, admin cross-agent listing, grouping and the attention filter, reviewer from identity, `NOT_PERMITTED` for an agentless rating, `RATING_CHANGED` / `RATING_NOT_FOUND`, bad requests, non-leaking internal errors. Mutation-checked: 8 of 8 mutants killed by a test failure (role tier widened, role gate dropped, guard refusal not mapped to FORBIDDEN, agentId / needsAttention / conversationId not forwarded, REST code lost, empty review accepted).
- `McpRoleTierParityTest`, `McpToolContractTest`, `McpToolFilterCoverageTest` and `SkillToolReferencesTest` updated (`ratingConfig`, `canOpenTranscript` exempted as config/result fields).
- Manager: operator `tool-scopes` and `system-prompt` tests for the grant, the excluded writes/export and the gated section.

## ✨ feat(httpcalls): `forEach` — one call per element, every result collected (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/step-checks-foreach`) — item E5 part A of the evaluated-generation plan

### What changed and why

`preRequest.batchRequests` only works with `fireAndForget`, so a call that needed one request per element of an array
threw every response away. An httpcall now takes an optional `forEach` block that sends the call once per element and
stores what each element produced — `index`, `ok`, `httpCode`, `body` or `error`, `timedOut`, `sent`, `attempts`,
`bodyDropped` — as one array under `responseObjectName`, in source order whatever order the items finished in.
`postResponse` runs once with `200` when every item succeeded and `207` (Multi-Status) when any failed, so property
instructions gated on the default codes only see complete results unless the author opts in.

- **New field, not an extension of `batchRequests`.** A stored config with `batchRequests` on a call that waits for its
  response sends exactly one request today; reusing the field would have turned it into N requests silently. Without
  `forEach` nothing changes, and `forEach` is refused (400) together with `fireAndForget` or `batchRequests`.
- **Bounded four ways.** `maxItems` (the batch default 100 and ceiling 1000, refused as a whole before anything is
  sent), `maxConcurrency` (default 1 — the load the same calls written one after another would send; at most 16, on
  virtual threads), the turn deadline per item (an item whose turn comes after it is not sent), and memory: each body
  is capped by `maxResponseSizeInBytes` and all of them together by `maxTotalResponseSizeInBytes` (default the same
  value, so the array gets what one response would).
- **Never abandon paid work.** A failing item never cancels its siblings; the pool is shut down, never interrupted;
  `onItemFailure: "fail"` fails the step only after every item finished and the results are stored. When the step stops
  waiting, queued items are not started at all, so nothing is sent whose result nobody collects.
- **A request that threw is never retried** — a timeout above all, since the API may have processed (and billed) it.
  `retryApiCallInstruction` still applies per item to responses.
- **Security unchanged.** Every item's request is built on the turn's thread by the same `buildRequest` a single call
  uses (templating, vault/connection/caller resolution, SSRF check, approval scope); only sending is parallel. The
  element is bound as template data, never template source. A `forEach` call is unpinnable for HITL fingerprints, like
  a fire-and-forget batch.
- **Seams left for parallel work.** `BoundedFanOut.Admission` is asked before each item starts and told when it
  finished — where the per-conversation cost reservation (E4) plugs in. The fan-out runner is generic, so mcpcalls can
  reuse it. The deploy-time turn-budget warning counts a `forEach` call once per round.

**Files:** [`ForEachInstruction.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/apicalls/model/ForEachInstruction.java),
[`ApiCall.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/apicalls/model/ApiCall.java),
[`ApiCallExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallExecutor.java),
[`ForEachItems.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ForEachItems.java),
[`ForEachItemResult.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ForEachItemResult.java),
[`BoundedFanOut.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/BoundedFanOut.java),
[`ApiCallsConfigurationValidator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/apicalls/ApiCallsConfigurationValidator.java),
[`RestApiCallsStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/apicalls/rest/RestApiCallsStore.java),
[`TurnResilienceWarnings.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/TurnResilienceWarnings.java),
[`docs/httpcalls.md`](../httpcalls.md) (also drops a duplicated "Turn deadline" section), the Platform Operator prompt
and revision.

### What's next

Part B of E5 — `checks` on httpcall and mcpcall results, reusing the output guardrail types — and `forEach` on
mcpcalls are not in this change; the plan is in the PR description.


### End-to-end proof

`ForEachHttpCallsIT` drives a deployed agent over REST against WireMock: one request per element (templated into path
and body, source order, read back from the stored conversation), a filter leaving index gaps, `maxItems` refusing the
whole call before anything is sent, the total-size budget counting UTF-8 bytes of a non-ASCII payload, an empty list
sending nothing, a 500 and a timeout not stopping or being retried, `207` gating of `postResponse`, `onItemFailure:
"fail"` running every item first, and the concurrency bound (1 by default, 2 when configured) observed from WireMock's
arrival times.

## ✨ feat(hitl,groups): humans and groups produce verdicts — revise with the note, rework within a run, stop on a verdict (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/verdict-rework`) — item E2 of `planning/evaluated-generation-plan.md`.

### What changed and why

Three places where a check already ran but its verdict could not repair anything now can, each off by default.

**1. Single-agent HITL: `hitlConfig.onRejection: "REVISE"`** (decision 9.5: a `hitlConfig` policy, not a new verdict value). Under the default `SKIP` a rejected rule pause skips the remaining tasks, as before. Under `REVISE`, a rejection **with a human's note** has the same model revise the answer it gave before the pause: one tool-less re-ask over the frozen request that produced the draft (system prompt, history, retrieved context, tool results), the draft as the assistant turn and the note as the user turn, then the pipeline continues from the task after the model's, so a rule that paused on the draft pauses again on the revision. `maxRevisionsPerTurn` (1..10, default 3) bounds it.
- Scope: behavior-rule (`RULE`) pauses only. A rejected tool call (`TOOL_CALL`) is a rejected action, not a rejected answer, and already reaches the model as a tool result with the note; it is unchanged. A rejection without a note, an automated one (`system:*`), a Slack button (its note is its own attribution), a pause before any answer, an answer from a later task or workflow, an over-cap request, a spend refusal, a model failure, a failed `responseValidation` or a guardrail block all fall back to `SKIP`, and `hitl:revision:outcome` records which.
- The note is data: concatenated into the user message, never template source. The revision passes `responseValidation` and the output guardrails and asks the spend gate first; its tokens and cost are recorded like any answer's.
- The candidate (`HitlRevisionCandidate`) is recorded by `LlmTask` only while the policy is on, stamped with its pipeline position by `LifecycleManager`, and persisted by the RULE pause as step data `hitl:revision:candidate` — so it travels through both stores unchanged, is scrubbed of secret input and context with the step, is withheld from `returnDetailed` and from the approver's full snapshot, and is removed when the pause is decided. Cap `eddi.hitl.revision.transcript-max-bytes` (256 KB). The round count rides on the candidate, so the bound holds across re-pauses and reloads.
- New seam: `IAnswerRevisingTask` (implemented by `LlmTask`) and `ILifecycleManager.reviseAnswerAt`, which runs one task's revision with its audit entry and converts a later task's gated tool call into a TOOL_CALL pause. Output that the draft and the tasks after it wrote is cut back before they run again.
- Records: `hitl.approval` carries `onRejection: REVISE`; meter `eddi_hitl_revision_total{outcome}`.

**2. TASK_FORCE `maxReworks`** (group field, 0..5, default 0). A task that fails VERIFY returns to `ASSIGNED` with the verifier's feedback (`SharedTaskList.reworkTask`, the same transition and feedback channel as `onTaskRejection: RETRY`), the nearest EXECUTE phase runs it again inside the VERIFY phase and VERIFY checks it again, up to `maxReworks` times per task. Stopped by `maxTurns` (a round needs one turn per task plus the verifier's) and the cost ceiling; a rework that could not run ends `FAILED`, never stranded. Not applied when EXECUTE gates tasks for human approval. Counts persist in `taskList.reworkCounts` (additive, no schema bump: an older pod re-saving drops them, which fails soft). An unparseable verdict keeps the fail-open auto-verify but is now warned and counted (`eddi_group_verify_unparseable_total`); reworks are `eddi_group_task_reworks_total`.

**3. `convergence.mode: "VERDICT"`** on a repeating phase. The moderator reviews the latest repeat against the question and the phase ends once it passes (`{"passed": true}` as a JSON boolean; anything else keeps it going). `threshold` is unused and `minRepeats` may be 1. `"SIMILARITY"` stays the default, and an unknown mode is a save-time 400.

**Compatibility:** stored agent and group configs load and behave identically — `onRejection` absent is `SKIP` and captures nothing, `maxReworks` absent is 0, `convergence.mode` absent is `SIMILARITY` with the old `minRepeats` floor. Old pods ignore the new fields (`FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES` is off). The approval API (REST `/resume`, MCP `resume_conversation`, Slack buttons, Manager approvals) is unchanged in shape.

**Manager:** the agent HITL section gains "When a reviewer rejects" and "Revisions per turn"; the phase editor gains a convergence "Stop when" select (VERDICT hides the threshold, floor 1); Task force settings gain "Reworks after a failed verification". All 11 locales. **Platform Operator:** a cheatsheet entry for the three settings; revision 36 → 37. Four other open branches (E1c, E4, E5, #1121) also bump `operator-revision.json`, so whichever lands second must re-bump and refresh the fingerprint.

**Docs:** [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md) (new "Revise with the reviewer's note"), [`group-conversations.md`](../group-conversations.md) (rework within a run, `convergence.mode`), [`llm-resilience.md`](../llm-resilience.md), [`metrics.md`](../metrics.md), [`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md); three dashboard panels, synced to the Kubernetes copy.

### Verification

New tests: `LlmTaskHitlRevisionTest`, `ConversationHitlRevisionTest` (including a resume after a JSON and a BSON round trip of the paused snapshot), `LifecycleManagerRevisionTest`, `HitlRejectionPolicyConfigTest`, `TaskForceEngineReworkTest`, `ConvergenceVerdictModeTest`, plus Manager tests for the three editors. Nine mutants (revision bound, default policy, note requirement, automated decisions, per-task rework bound, `maxReworks` default, rework turn budget, convergence default mode, boolean-only verdict) are each killed.
- End-to-end ITs on MongoDB and PostgreSQL (`HitlRevisionIT`, `GroupVerdictIT` and their `postgres/` twins), with WireMock as the model: a rejection with a note revises over the frozen request (the note and the draft reach the model, the candidate and its round count survive the store across both pauses, approval delivers the revision only), `no_note` and `exhausted` skip; VERDICT convergence stops on a JSON `"passed": true` while prose, a string `"true"` or a failing judge run every repeat, and an unknown mode is a 400; TASK_FORCE `maxReworks` sends a failed task back with the verifier's feedback and persists `taskList.reworkCounts`. Not persisting the candidate, dropping the rework feedback and never passing a verdict each fail them.
- **Fix — the raw read leaked the frozen request.** `GET /conversationstore/conversations/{id}` (the owner's and the Platform Operator's raw read) served the paused step's `hitl:revision:candidate` whole, so its `transcriptJson` — the compiled system prompt and every tool result — reached anyone allowed to read the conversation, in `result` and again in the `possibleResults` copy. `ConversationMemoryUtilities.redactRawPendingToolCallsForRead`, already the raw surface's single redaction point for the tool-call batch, now also withholds the candidate's frozen input (`REVISION_CANDIDATE_FROZEN_INPUT_FIELDS`) on every step and the redo cache, keeping the draft and bookkeeping. The other surfaces were already safe: `returnDetailed` (REST, MCP `read_conversation`, the Manager's inspector and the `/simple` read) drops the key, `approval-status?detail=full` (REST and MCP) removes the candidate, and the GDPR export carries only `conversationOutputs`. `ConversationMemoryUtilitiesRevisionRedactionTest`; `HitlRevisionIT` asserts no candidate entry carries the transcript or the system prompt while the revision request still carries it. **Not changed, and older than E2:** the raw read still serves `audit:compiled_prompt`, the LLM task's own audit step data (written on every answered turn while the audit ledger is on), which holds the same compiled system prompt; the IT pins that this is the only place it appears. Whether the raw read should drop `audit:*` like `returnDetailed` does is a separate decision. `hitl.md` now says `hitlDecision` reads `REJECTED` only until the revision is decided.

### What's next

The judge machinery of E1 (#1121) could serve as VERIFY's and VERDICT's verdict parser, and the agent-testing harness's LLM-as-judge expectation should reuse it. A failing VERDICT review is recorded but not shown to the members of the next repeat; feeding it back is a follow-up.

## 📝 docs(planning): white-labeling plan for every UI (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/white-label-ui`)

### What changed and why

Added [`planning/white-label-ui-plan.md`](../../planning/white-label-ui-plan.md): an inventory of every place the Manager, the Chat UI, the backend-served pages, the Platform Operator and the Keycloak login theme show the EDDI brand, and a phased design to replace it with an admin-defined brand without a rebuild or restart.

### Design decisions

- The brand is one database document edited on a Manager **Settings → Branding** page. `eddi.branding.*` properties seed it, and `eddi.branding.locked=true` makes them authoritative for GitOps installs.
- Delivery stays same-origin: fixed `/branding/*` asset URLs with a bundled fallback, a runtime `/branding/__brand__.js` plus `/branding/brand.css`, and serve-time `<head>` substitution in the HTML shells. The CSP does not change.
- Technical identifiers (`eddi.*` properties, `X-EDDI-*` headers, storage keys, postMessage types) are contracts and are not renamed.
- "Powered by EDDI" is switchable and on by default. The update check stays opt-in, and its UI is hidden when branded. The docs link is parked.
- The branding endpoints are deliberately left off the Platform Operator's allow-list.

### Next

Phase 1 of the plan, the backend brand store and delivery.

---

## 🎨 feat(branding): brand store, admin REST and same-origin delivery — white-label phase 1 (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/white-label-ui`)

### What changed and why

Phase 1 of the [white-labeling plan](../../planning/white-label-ui-plan.md): the backend brand store and its delivery to every UI. With no brand set, nothing visible changes. The page is [White-Labeling](../white-labeling.md).

- **Brand store.** `configs/branding`: one `BrandDocument` per deployment, behind `IBrandingStore`. It has a MongoDB `BrandingStore` (collections `branding` and `brandingassets`) and a `PostgresBrandingStore` (tables `branding` and `branding_assets`), picked in `DataStoreProducers`. Images are stored per slot (`logo`, `logoDark`, `icon`, `favicon`, `ogImage`, a closed enum); the document holds only their `sha256:` hashes. Writes are compare-and-set on the revision.
- **`BrandingConfig`** (`@ConfigMapping`, `eddi.branding.*`) seeds every field the stored document leaves unset. With `locked=true` the properties are authoritative.
- **`BrandingService`** is the single entry point (`current(BrandRequest)`). It resolves locked → document → seed → defaults field by field, and validates writes and the seed with one validator (`BrandValidator`, `BrandAssetValidator`); an invalid seed fails startup naming the property. It caches for 30 s with immediate local invalidation, answers 409 on a stale revision or when locked, and writes an INFO audit line.
- **Admin REST** `IRestBrandingAdmin` at `/administration/branding` (`eddi-admin`): `GET`, `PUT`, `DELETE`, and `PUT`/`DELETE /assets/{slot}`.
- **Public delivery** `IRestBranding` at `/branding/*` (`@PermitAll`, hidden from OpenAPI): the five images with a bundled fallback, plus `__brand__.js` and `brand.css`. `/branding/*` joins the static-assets permit list and the micrometer path patterns.
- **Head substitution.** The five shells (`manage`, `welcome`, `workforce`, `chat`, root `index.html`) carry `%BRAND_PRODUCT_NAME%`, `%BRAND_TAGLINE%` and `%BRAND_THEME_COLOR%`, `/branding/*` favicons, the brand script and the brand stylesheet. `BrandedShellRenderer` fills them in, HTML-escaped and cached per (shell, revision), for the four shell resources. `BrandedShellRoute`, a Vert.x route, does the same for the static `/`, `/index.html` and `/<shell>.html` paths.
- **One JS-string escaper,** `JsGlobalScript`. `__auth_config__.js` and `/chat/__config__.js` moved onto it with byte-identical output.
- **Dev server.** `scripts/vite-plugin-brand.mjs` in both UIs fills the defaults and serves `/branding/*` under `npm run dev` and MSW mode. A test in each UI runs a real `vite build` over the real shells and checks that the placeholders survive and that `brand.css` comes after the bundle's stylesheet. The MSW passthrough list gained `*/branding/*`.
- **Docs:** `docs/white-labeling.md`, a White-labeling section in `configuration-reference.md`, and `SUMMARY.md`. `ConfigurationReferenceCoverageTest` now reads `@ConfigMapping` interfaces, so mapped properties are held to the same documented-and-real rule as `@ConfigProperty` ones. `ReservedPrincipalsTest` classifies `sha256:` (the image hash) as a non-principal prefix.
- **Verified through the persistence boundary.** `BrandingStoreMongoIT` and `BrandingStorePostgresIT` share one contract (`BrandingStoreContract`). Their first run found `isEmpty()` serialized as an `"empty"` property that the reader then refused, so every stored brand was unreadable; `BrandingJsonTest` now pins the round trip without Docker.

### Design decisions

- **Field-wise merge, and a monotonic revision.** A `null` in the document means "the seed's value, else EDDI's". Reset writes an empty document under the next revision rather than deleting it, so caches keyed on the revision can never serve a stale page.
- **`brand.css` is linked as the first child of `<body>`, not in `<head>`.** Vite injects the bundle's stylesheet at the end of `<head>`, so a `<head>` link would come first and lose to the bundle's `.dark` block. The build test pins the order.
- **Per-shell default taglines** (`BrandedShell`) keep every unbranded description byte-identical. The welcome and chat descriptions differed before branding existed.
- **`/branding/favicon.ico` for the chat shell,** which already used an ICO. The favicon falls back to the icon, and the dark logo and og-image fall back to the logo, so a brand with only a logo and an icon leaves no EDDI image behind.
- **The SVG filter is stricter than the plan.** On top of `<script>`, `<foreignObject>`, `on…=`, `javascript:` and external `href`, it refuses namespace-prefixed elements, `<animate>`/`<set>`, entity declarations, character references (which would hide `javascript:` from a text check), `@import` and external CSS `url()`.
- **`brand.css` also sets `--color-sidebar-accent-foreground`**, so the sidebar accent keeps the brand's contrast pair.
- **Not on the Platform Operator's allow-list** (plan R9). `openapi-operations.json` gains the five admin operations.

### What's next

Phases 2–7 of the plan, starting with the Manager token cleanup.

## 🐛 fix(agent-tests): mocked httpcall tools keep their postResponse, runs can be cancelled, replicas stop aborting each other's runs (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/agent-tests-fidelity`)

Review follow-ups to the agent regression harness (PR #1065).

### What changed and why

- **An httpcall mocked on the LLM tool path now runs its response handling.**
  `ToolLoopRunner` answered every mocked LLM tool call itself, before any executor,
  so for an httpcall tool `saveResponse`, retries and `postResponse` property
  instructions never ran: an agent that sets a property from a tool's response
  behaved differently mocked than live, and its `properties` expectations failed.
  The call is still decided and recorded once in `ToolLoopRunner`
  (`ToolMockRegistry.decideToolCall`); a `MOCKED` decision for an executor that
  implements the new `MockAwareToolExecutor` (the httpcall tools of
  `HttpCallToolsProvider`) is handed to it, and `ApiCallExecutor` answers the call
  with it — no request built — exactly as it already did for a rule-triggered call.
  Refusals (unmocked, fail-closed) and every other tool source short-circuit as
  before. A mocked call is uncached and unpriced.
- **A retried mocked call is recorded once.** `ApiCallExecutor` intercepted inside
  the retry loop, so each retry was recorded again and counted against
  `maxToolCalls` (and a retried unmocked call produced several `unmockedTool`
  failures). The decision is made once per call, as a retried live call is one call.
- **Cancel.** `POST /agenttests/runs/{runId}/cancel` and the MCP tool
  `cancel_agent_test_run` (author tier, EDIT on the agent — what starting one
  takes) stop a run cooperatively: no further turn starts, a waiting turn is
  abandoned, its conversations are ended and deleted, and the run ends with the new
  status `CANCELLED`. Before, a run of up to 2,000 turns × 900 s held one of two run
  slots until the node restarted. Manager: a **Cancel run** button on a running
  run, the `CANCELLED` badge and notice (11 locales).
- **Orphan recovery is owner- and heartbeat-aware.** At startup every `RUNNING` run
  was marked `ABORTED` and its conversations deleted — wrong when several replicas
  share one database without cluster mode. A run now records `ownerNode`
  (`<nodeId>/<bootId>`) and refreshes `heartbeatAt` every 30 s; a node aborts only a
  run of an earlier boot of itself or one whose heartbeat is older than three
  minutes, at startup and every three minutes.
- **Capacity before the deploy.** `AgentTestService` checked the run slots after
  deploying the version to `test`, so a request refused with 429 had already
  changed what the `test` environment serves.
- **Reserved test user ids.** A conversation started from outside as
  `agent-test:<liveRunId>:c:r` joined that run's mock session and wrote into its
  results. `OwnershipValidator.validateAndResolveUserId` now refuses any requested
  user id in that namespace (403), for an admin and with authorization off too.
- **A permanent delete removes memory checkpoints.** `ConversationPurgeService.purge`
  (every permanent conversation delete, not only test runs) left the checkpoints the
  tool loop writes before every tool call; only GDPR erasure removed them.
- **PostgreSQL IT and a two-turn case.** `PostgresAgentTestSuiteIT` runs the harness
  IT against PostgreSQL; `AgentTestSuiteIT` gained a two-turn case.
- **Docs.** `agent-testing.md`: the Manager page is shipped (not a follow-up), the
  "a mocked httpcall is still built" limitation was wrong and is replaced, cancel and
  the liveness rules are documented, and test conversations are noted to appear in
  listings and metrics while a run is in progress.

### Design decisions

- The mock rides into `ApiCallExecutor` in the template data
  (`ToolMockRegistry.TOOL_PATH_MOCK`), next to the existing `TOOL_PATH_MARKER`,
  rather than through a `ThreadLocal`: the executor runs on the tool-timeout thread.
- Cancel is node-local. A cancel arriving at another replica answers 409 naming the
  owner; a cross-node flag would race the heartbeat's whole-document writes.
- A run recorded before 6.6 (no owner, no heartbeat) counts from its `startedAt`.

**Files:** [`ToolLoopRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java), [`MockAwareToolExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/MockAwareToolExecutor.java), [`HttpCallToolsProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/HttpCallToolsProvider.java), [`ApiCallExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallExecutor.java), [`ToolMockRegistry.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/ToolMockRegistry.java), [`AgentTestRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestRunner.java), [`AgentTestService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestService.java), [`AgentTestRunRecovery.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestRunRecovery.java), [`ConversationPurgeService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationPurgeService.java), [`OwnershipValidator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/OwnershipValidator.java), [`agent-testing.md`](../agent-testing.md)

## 🐛 fix(agent-tests): review follow-ups — skew-proof orphan detection, CAS heartbeats, reserved ids at schedules (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/agent-tests-fidelity`)

### What changed and why

- **Recovery can no longer abort a live run.** Staleness compared another node's
  `heartbeatAt` with the local clock (clock skew or a short write outage aborted a
  live run and deleted its conversations), and the "earlier boot of this node" rule
  needed no heartbeat at all — two EDDI processes on one host share a host-derived
  node id and aborted each other's runs. Now a run is an orphan only when its
  heartbeat has not *changed* across this node's own sweeps for 3 minutes (90 s for
  the same node id, another boot), timed on the observer's monotonic clock; the
  sweep runs every minute.
- **Heartbeat, abort and final write are compare-and-swaps on `heartbeatAt`**
  (`IAgentTestRunStore.updateIfHeartbeat`, `storeIfFieldEquals`). An abort clears
  the heartbeat; the owner's next beat then fails, and the owner stops the run and
  writes nothing back — the record no longer flips from `ABORTED` to `RUNNING`.
- **Abandoned turns.** A turn given up on (timeout or cancel) can finish after its
  conversation was deleted; once it completes, the conversation is deleted again.
- **Tool calls are recorded after the loop's gates.** A mocked httpcall tool (or a
  live call) refused by the tool budget, tenant quota or turn deadline no longer
  counts as a call (`ToolMockRegistry.previewToolCall` / `recordToolCall`).
- **Reserved `agent-test:` ids** are also refused at REST schedule save and at
  every schedule fire — including a persistent schedule that reuses its stored
  conversation and a Dream consolidation fire, which never start one — and
  `OwnershipValidator` checks the resolved id as well as the requested one.
- **A non-`MockResult` under the tool-path mock key never mocks**: the call is
  treated as undecided — refused in a test conversation, sent in any other.
- **Permanent delete removes HITL tool-journal entries** too, as GDPR erasure does.
- **Platform Operator** may cancel a run (`POST /agenttests/runs/{runId}/cancel`,
  gated) and knows the terminal statuses; revision 37.
- **i18n:** `ABORTED` is "interrupted" in de, es, pt, hi, th and ar, so it no longer
  reads the same as `CANCELLED`.

**Files:** [`AgentTestRunRecovery.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestRunRecovery.java), [`AgentTestRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestRunner.java), [`AgentTestRunStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestRunStore.java), [`ToolLoopRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java), [`ScheduleFireExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/internal/ScheduleFireExecutor.java), [`ConversationPurgeService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationPurgeService.java), [`agent-testing.md`](../agent-testing.md)

## 🐛 fix(tenancy): the cost gates fail closed on both datastores, and undo no longer refunds spend (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/cost-gate-consistency`)

### What changed and why

Follow-ups to the tenant monthly cost budget and the per-conversation cost ceiling.

- **The pre-call cost gate answered a store outage differently per backend.** `PostgresTenantQuotaStore.getMonthlyCost` caught the `SQLException` and returned `0.0`, so the gate read "nothing spent" and allowed every model and tool call (fail open). `MongoTenantQuotaStore.getMonthlyCost` did not catch the `MongoException`, and `LlmTask` asks `LlmSpendGate` outside its guarded model phase, so the turn failed instead of serving the fallback answer. [`tenant-quotas.md`](../tenant-quotas.md) promised fail closed. Both stores now raise `QuotaAccountingUnavailableException`, as their `getQuota` already did ([`ITenantQuotaStore`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/tenancy/ITenantQuotaStore.java) documents the contract), and [`TenantQuotaService.checkCostBudget`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/tenancy/TenantQuotaService.java) turns it into a refusal flagged `accountingUnavailable`, counted on `eddi.tenant.quota.unavailable{type=cost}`. An exception that still escapes the check is a defect, not an outage. [`LlmSpendGate`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/LlmSpendGate.java) and the tool-call gate in [`ToolLoopRunner`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java) both refuse it (the fallback answer, a tool error result) under a separate reason, `tenant_check_error`. It is counted on `eddi.llm.spend.refused` and logged at ERROR, with the stack trace at most once a minute. It does not pass for an outage on `eddi.tenant.quota.unavailable`.
- **say → undo → say discarded spend.** The ceiling summed `audit:cost` over the live steps. Undo moved the priced step to the redo cache, and the next say cleared it, so a public `/undo` reset what a conversation had spent. [`ConversationMemory.startNextStep()`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationMemory.java) now carries the discarded steps' spend onto the new step as `audit:discarded_cost`, a new key in [`MemoryKeys`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/MemoryKeys.java) that is persisted with the step. The ceiling counts `audit:discarded_cost` and the redo cache, which a rerun runs against. The snapshot's conversation total counts both as well, so it no longer drops between an undo and the next say; it is read by the Manager, the group cost ledger, Slack and schedule deltas. Redo leaves the total unchanged, and a rerun already added to the step's own `audit:cost`.
- **Undo and redo kept the wrong `audit:pending_cost`.** `TurnCostMeter` reads the owed tenant charge from the newest step that has one, and undo and redo reorder those steps. An undone step's pending charge was lost on the next say. A rerun after an undo re-charged an amount the undone step had already recovered. A rerun's own outage charge was then shadowed by the undone or redone step's older value. Undo and redo now copy the pending value of the step they move aside onto the step that becomes current, `0.0` included. That step always holds the newer of the two values, and both writes are full replaces, so the copy is persisted.
- **Dream charges lost to an outage** are carried in [`DreamService`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/internal/DreamService.java) (node-local, in-process) and added to the next priced consolidation charge. The docs say a restart loses it.
- **Manager quotas page.** The cost-budget status checked "unlimited" before "enforced", so a disabled tenant with no budget was told its spend was metered, although `recordCost` records nothing while enforcement is off. Enforcement is checked first, with a new `quotas.budgetDisabled` message in all 11 locales. Amounts under $1 keep up to four decimals, so $0.006 against a $0.01 cap no longer reads "$0.01 of $0.01 … $0.00 left".
- `TurnCostMeterTest`'s outage test claimed the marker is left untouched. It advances, as the class documents, and the test now says so and asserts it.
- `ReservedPrincipalsTest` (inherited from main) declared the `"sha256:"` prefix twice, once for brand image hashes and once for mail Message-IDs. `Map.ofEntries` rejects duplicate keys, so the class failed to initialise. The two entries are now one, whose description covers both uses.

### Decisions

- `LlmSpendGate.countMidLoop` stays on `Metrics.globalRegistry`. Its caller, `ToolLoopRunner`, is not a CDI bean and is built without a registry in `AgentOrchestrator` and a dozen tests. That is the same idiom as `LifecycleManager`, and Quarkus adds its registry to the global composite. The Javadoc now says so.
- The old-JSON round trip for `maxCostPerConversationUsd` already existed (`ConversationCostCeilingTest.storedConfigCompatibility`), so no test was added for it.
- Not added: an IT of the full deployment path, from a priced model through `ConversationService` to a refused tool call. The existing IT scaffolding has no priced model to drive through the service. The store-level halves are covered for both datastores in `TenantCostMeteringIT`: a monthly-cost read that fails at the gate, and say → undo → say persisted through the real conversation store.

**Docs:** [`tenant-quotas.md`](../tenant-quotas.md), [`langchain.md`](../langchain.md#per-conversation-llm-cost-ceiling), [`metrics.md`](../metrics.md).

---

## 🔒 fix(guardrails): a blocked message no longer reaches the model on the next turn (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/guardrail-history-replay`)

### What changed and why

Review of the input/output guardrails (#1064, #1112) found that a message the `classifier` guardrail **blocked** was sent to the main model on the following turn. An input block is served as a fallback; the history builder merges a fallback turn's question into the next user message so roles keep alternating; input guardrails treat that merged text as history, and history is only re-screened by guardrails with `screensHistory() == true` — which the classifier is not (one model call per history message would multiply its cost). So: an injection blocked on turn N, then "hi" on turn N+1, and the model received the injection. The same path undid a classifier `redact`.

- **A durable marker, not re-screening.** When an input guardrail blocks — or redacts with a guardrail that does not re-screen history — `LlmTask` flags the step `llm:input_withheld:<taskId>` (`MemoryKeys.LLM_INPUT_WITHHELD_PREFIX`). `ConversationLogGenerator.isInputWithheldStep` reads it (and, for steps written before it existed, an `audit:guardrail:input:*` record with action `block`). Both history paths (`ConversationLogGenerator.generate` with `skipFallbackOutputs`, `ConversationHistoryBuilder.generateMessagesFromOutputs`) replace that turn's user message — text, extracts and input files — with `[earlier message withheld by a guardrail]`, for every guardrail type. The rolling summarizer's input and the recall tool get the same view through `ConversationLogGenerator.modelFacingOutputs`. Memory and the human transcript keep the message.
- **Current-turn text is located structurally.** `AttachmentForwarder` appends extract/note text parts behind the typed text, so "the last text part" used to be the extract: a first-pass redaction was not applied to what the model saw, and the classifier screened the extract instead of the typed text. `LlmTask` now records the typed text's content index before forwarding; `GuardrailService.runInput` applies the (reused) first-pass result to it and screens each attachment text part after it as this turn's input — recorded, able to block.
- **Bounded author regexes.** `regex-filter` and `prompt-injection` patterns run through a shared deadline-checking matcher (the classifier `labelPattern` one, later folded into `RegexDeadline`) with 250 ms per text; past it the guardrail throws `GuardrailUndecidedException("pattern_timeout")`, so `failMode` applies — a `block` guardrail refuses the text instead of passing it. Both types now `validate()` their patterns, so an invalid one fails the deploy instead of being dropped (which left a block-only filter that never fires).
- **Spend gate first.** The spend gate is asked before the input guardrails and retrieval, so an over-budget turn pays no classifier call or embedding query; every guardrail reprompt asks it again.
- **`postResponse` after an input block.** It now runs, as after every other fallback (output block, spend refusal, `onError`), so a `convertToObject` + `addToOutput: false` task whose `postResponse` builds the reply answers the user instead of showing nothing — and the docs' claim that the `fallbackField` shape is what `postResponse` reads is now true for both sides.
- **History-screening cost** is recorded whether or not the guardrail fires.

### Review follow-ups

- **A withheld turn's files stay out too.** `AttachmentContextExtractor.attachmentsFromPreviousTurns` skips withheld steps (no re-inlined image, no "files shared earlier" note), and `ReadAttachmentTool` is built with `withheldStorageRefs` so `listAttachments`/`readAttachment` cannot hand a blocked file's content back as an unscreened tool result.
- **The current turn is not withheld by its own flag** — a second LLM task of the same turn decides it with its own guardrails (both history builders skip the last output index).
- **Spend gate asked twice**: before the input guardrails and again after them, so a classifier's price cannot carry the model call past a ceiling. A turn refused before screening is flagged withheld when a configured input guardrail does not re-screen history (it was never classified).
- **One regex deadline helper.** `BoundedRegex` is folded into `ai.labs.eddi.utils.RegexDeadline` (now with a deadline-preserving `subSequence` and `matcher(...)`); [`security.md`](../security.md#parser-and-matcher-bounds) lists the guardrail patterns' bound. A pattern timeout in a `regex-filter` keeps what the finished patterns found (`GuardrailUndecidedException.partialFinding`): failing open, a `redact` keeps those rewrites and a `block` that matched still blocks.
- **Docs**: template data (`{memory.last.input}`, `{memory.past}`, `{conversationLog}`, captured properties) is *not* filtered — don't template earlier user input into a guarded task's prompt or `httpCall` body; a turn on which the guarded task did not run was never classified.
- Persistence round trip and undo/redo of the flag through the real `ConversationMemory` (`InputWithheldRoundTripTest`); the Platform Operator's guardrail paragraph learned the pattern budget, the deploy-time pattern check and the withheld placeholder.

**Deferred:** a reprompted answer is still not re-run through `responseValidation` (its re-ask loop does not return to the output guardrails, so wiring the two together is not a small change); documented in [`langchain.md`](../langchain.md#where-guardrails-run).

**Files:** [`GuardrailService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/GuardrailService.java), [`LlmTask.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/LlmTask.java), [`ConversationLogGenerator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationLogGenerator.java), [`ConversationHistoryBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ConversationHistoryBuilder.java), [`RegexDeadline.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/utils/RegexDeadline.java), `RegexFilterGuardrail`, `PromptInjectionGuardrail`, `PatternCache`, `ClassifierGuardrail`, `ConversationSummarizer`, `ContextualToolsProvider`, `MemoryKeys`; docs [`langchain.md`](../langchain.md#input-and-output-guardrails).

## 🐛 fix(webhook): the replay-protection cache could evict live keys, was per node, and a few edges leaked or orphaned (2026-10-24)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/webhook-dedup-replay`)

### What changed and why

Review of the signed inbound webhook channel (#1059) found that its de-duplication - the only replay protection of the `github` and `generic-hmac` schemes, and a second line for `eddi-v1` - was weaker than documented.

- **Dedup cache sizing and admission.** `webhook-event-dedup` had no size entry in `CacheFactory`, so it inherited the 1,000-entry default and was shared by every webhook integration, each delivery storing up to two keys. About 500 deliveries in ten minutes from any one integration evicted another's keys, after which a captured request ran again. The cache now has its own entry and is rate-sized like the A2A nonce cache (100 keys per second over the 10-minute window, factor-two head-room: 120,000 entries), and the handler **refuses instead of evicting** (an evicted key is a replayable request, a refused delivery is only a retry), per integration so that one sender cannot push the others out:
  - Each integration has its own budget, `platformConfig.maxDeliveriesPerWindow` (default 3000 deliveries per 10 minutes, validated 1 to 100000 at save), counted over a rolling window of 11 one-minute buckets (longer than the 10-minute key lifetime, so a delivery is counted as long as its keys exist). A delivery that is not accepted (429, 503, ...) gives its slot back, so a sender looping on a quota refusal keeps seeing the real refusal. The counters are a bounded Caffeine cache that expires idle integrations.
  - From half capacity, an integration above its fair share (half the cache divided by the active integrations, counting only those with 50 or more deliveries in the window, so throwaway integrations cannot shrink anyone's share) is refused even inside its own budget.
  - From 90% of capacity every new delivery is refused, whatever its integration holds - a last resort that keeps the cache clear of eviction.
  - Repeats of remembered deliveries are always answered, and the repeat check runs only when a refusal is pending, so an admitted delivery costs no extra cache read (in a cluster, no extra NATS round trip).
  - Refusals are their own metric outcome, `refused_capacity` (`metrics.md`, dashboard), and the `WARN` is limited to once a minute per integration. `ICache.size()` stays a cheap estimate; the new `ICache.sizeExact()` sweeps expired entries and is read only once the estimate reaches the threshold, so a refusal cannot outlast the data.
  - In a cluster the budgets and the thresholds are per node: the counters are node-local and the cache size read is the node-local view (documented).
- **Cluster.** `webhook-event-dedup` joins `slack-event-dedup` in the shared `DEDUP` NATS KV bucket (same 10-minute TTL), so a replay is caught on whichever node it hits. With NATS unreachable each node falls back to its own cache, as for Slack.
- **Unsigned routing headers.** Binary-mode `ce-type`/`ce-id` and the plain-mode event-type header are outside every scheme's MAC. The replay key is already derived from the MAC alone, so within the window the same signed body is a duplicate whatever header it arrives with (now covered by tests at handler and endpoint level); the page now says plainly that routing headers are unsigned, what that means (tampering in transit can re-route a body without breaking the signature) and what to do (TLS, route on a field of the signed body). No wire-protocol change and no new signing scheme.
- **Orphaned conversations.** A `429` (or any refusal) from the turn after `startConversation` left one empty conversation per retried delivery. The conversation just created is now ended when its first turn is refused; a correlated conversation is kept, since the retry reuses it.
- **Weak or plaintext signing secrets.** Save-time validation already requires a vault reference, but a ZIP import or direct store write bypasses it, and no length was checked anywhere. The router now refuses to hand the verifier a plaintext secret, a reference that did not resolve (left unchanged by resolution, also with surrounding whitespace) or a resolved one shorter than 16 characters; the verifier independently rejects anything still containing a `${vault:` reference (the integration answers `401`, with one `WARN` per integration).
- **Id probing.** An unknown integration id answered `404` before the signature was looked at. It now answers `401` with the same body as a bad signature (the log keeps the distinction). The Slack endpoint likewise answers a uniform `403` for anything it cannot verify.
- **Two meanings of `409`.** The body now carries `reason`: `in_progress` (retry in 5 seconds) or `awaiting_approval` (a human has to decide). The status code is unchanged.
- **Behaviour change for 6.6.0 installs:** a webhook integration whose vault secret is shorter than 16 characters used to work and now answers `401` to every request (as does a plaintext secret that reached the store through import). Find them with the log line "signing secret is shorter than" (one WARN per integration version, repeated after a vault secret change) and rotate to 32 random bytes. Saving such a secret is now refused with 400 when it already exists in the default-tenant vault; references to another tenant are not resolved at save time (that would let any editor probe other tenants' secrets), so the router's runtime check is what covers them.
- The Platform Operator prompt now knows the new answers (unknown id 401, weak secret 401, capacity 503, 409 `reason`), operator revision bumped.
- Group targets still receive only the rendered input, not the event map; documented, not changed.

Files: [`CacheFactory.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/caching/CacheFactory.java), [`WebhookEventHandler.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/webhook/WebhookEventHandler.java), [`RestWebhookChannel.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/webhook/rest/RestWebhookChannel.java), [`ChannelTargetRouter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/channels/ChannelTargetRouter.java), [`webhook-triggers.md`](../webhook-triggers.md).

### Design decisions

- No new property: the capacity is derived from the window like the nonce cache's, and the refusal threshold follows from it. A property would let an operator size the cache below the traffic it must hold, which is the defect being fixed.
- The check for a quota refusal before creating the conversation was rejected: the API-call quota has only an atomic acquire, no read-only peek, and adding one would race. Ending the orphan is the safe equivalent (each attempt still spends a conversation slot).
- The shared bucket is not size-capped and the node-local cache that stands in for it is, so in a cluster the thresholds read each node's own view: a sender spread over N nodes may hold N times its budget in total. Reading the exact cluster-wide size is not possible (the bucket cannot enumerate its keys).
- Per-integration admission rather than a global refusal: a global threshold let one integration with a valid secret push every other integration to 503. The fair share counts only integrations with 50+ deliveries because the roles that can create integrations (`eddi-admin`, `eddi-editor`) could otherwise shrink everyone's share with many one-delivery integrations.

### Tests

- `WebhookEventHandlerTest`: an integration over its budget gets 503 while another still gets 202 (and a remembered repeat is answered); the window rolls over after 11 minutes and a delivery still counts at 10; from half full only the integration above its fair share is refused; the 90% hard stop refuses an idle integration; throwaway one-delivery integrations do not shrink another's share; an admitted delivery performs no extra cache read; refused deliveries do not spend the budget; the WARN is throttled to once a minute; `refused_capacity` is its own outcome; the refused turn ends its new conversation with its own reason; both `409` reasons; the same signed body under another event type is a duplicate.
- `RestWebhookChannelTest`: unknown or malformed id answers identically to a bad signature; rerouted replay is a duplicate; `reason` in the body and no `Retry-After` for `awaiting_approval`.
- `CacheFactoryTest`: explicit, window-derived size; shared through the `DEDUP` bucket with the matching TTL. `CacheImplTest`: `sizeExact()` ignores expired entries.
- `ChannelTargetRouterWebhookTest`: plaintext and 15-character secrets are stripped, a 16-character one is kept; the weak-secret warning is logged once per refresh cycle and again after a vault change.
- `RestChannelIntegrationStoreValidationTest`: a short existing vault secret is refused at save, other-tenant references are never resolved, `maxDeliveriesPerWindow` is validated.

## 🐛 fix(ci): publish the `-b` image for a merge that does not touch `ui/` (2026-10-21)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/ci-docker-push-backend-only`)

### What changed and why

Since the monorepo split of 2026-09-15 (`4f19e4767`, "run each half only when it changed"), no merge to `main` that changed only backend code published a `-b` image. Build & Test, the integration tests and the E2E runs on the built image all passed, and **Docker Build & Push** was skipped.

The cause is GitHub's implicit `success()`. A job condition without a status function gets one added, and for a job with `needs` it grades every job upstream, not only the listed ones. Docker waits on `ui-gate`, which is written to pass when the UI did not change (`always()`, skipped ui-* jobs accepted). On a backend-only push those ui-* jobs are skipped, so the implicit `success()` of docker was false and it was skipped — and with it Smoke Test and Preflight Verify (Pushed Image), which depend on it. Of the last 25 merges, every backend-only one was affected; tag builds were not, because detect-changes forces `ui=true` for a tag.

[`ci.yml`](../../.github/workflows/ci.yml):
- `docker` starts its condition with `!cancelled()` and grades its own `needs` explicitly: no `failure`, `cancelled` or `skipped` result. That keeps the gate the job's NOTE insists on — every listed job must have succeeded, so a failed or skipped test job still blocks the push, and no `always()` is used.
- `smoke-test` gets `!cancelled() && needs.docker.result == 'success'`, and `preflight-push` gains `!cancelled()` in front of its existing condition, for the same reason.

The tag-only jobs (release, Red Hat publish, post-release) are unchanged: on a tag the ui-* jobs always run.

### Verification

- The history check behind the diagnosis — Docker push outcome against the ui-* jobs for the last 25 `main` pushes — lines up one to one.
- `BuildQualityGatesTest#jobsBehindUiGateGradeTheirNeedsExplicitly` fails on any job behind `ui-gate` that runs on a non-tag push without a status function, and on a docker condition that loses its explicit `needs` checks or gains `always()`. Against the old `ci.yml` it fails, naming docker, smoke-test and preflight-push.
- The merge of this branch does **not** prove the fix: it changes `ci.yml`, which the `ui` path filter counts as a UI change, so its ui-* jobs run and it would publish anyway. The proof is the next backend-only push to `main` — it must publish a `-b` image and run Smoke Test.

## Fix: `eddi update` on a pre-#1034 Postgres install (2026-10-20)

**Repo:** EDDI, branch `fix/update-backfill-postgres-password`

An install made before `docker-compose.postgres-only.yml` required `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` has no such line in `.env`. `eddi update` refreshed the compose file, `docker compose pull` and `up` then failed to interpolate it, and the Windows wrapper still printed "EDDI updated."

- `install.sh` and `install.ps1` (the `eddi` wrapper): after refreshing the compose files, if one references `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` and `.env` lacks it, write the legacy `eddi` value and print a note. A fresh random value would lock EDDI out of the existing volume, because Postgres reads the variable only at initdb.
- `install.ps1` wrapper: `update` now stops with exit code 1 when `pull` or `up -d` fails instead of reporting success.
- New installs are unaffected: both installers already generate the password.

## 🐛 fix(hitl): a resume could not re-supply secret context, because loading a conversation dropped `secret` (2026-10-20)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/secret-context-survives-store-load`)

### What changed and why

Secret context on resume (#1114) refused every resume that re-supplied a secret with 400 — `context['userToken'] was not a secret context value in the paused turn` — although the paused step was stored with `"secret": true` and the placeholder. Both conversation stores rebuild a stored `context:*` entry as `new Context(type, value)` when they load a conversation, which dropped the flag, and `ConversationHitlService.validateResumeContext` and `Conversation.injectResumeSecretContext` both read it from that loaded memory. A resume always loads from the store, so the feature could not work in a deployment; the tests of #1114 and #1116 build the memory in process and never went through a load.

Both stores now keep the flag when they rebuild the entry. Nothing else changes: the stored value is still the placeholder, the re-supplied value lives for the resumed turn only, and that turn stores the placeholder again.

Files: [`ConversationMemoryStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationMemoryStore.java) (`loadConversationMemorySnapshot`), [`PostgresConversationMemoryStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/datastore/postgres/PostgresConversationMemoryStore.java) (`fixContextTypes`).

### Tests

- `MongoConversationMemoryStoreTest`, `PostgresConversationMemoryStoreTest`: a secret entry keeps `secret: true` and the placeholder through a real store round trip; an ordinary entry stays unmarked.
- `ConversationMemoryStoreResilienceTest`: the same on the MongoDB load path with a mocked collection, so it also runs without Docker.
- Without the fix all three new tests fail (`expected: <true> but was: <null>`).
- End to end against an image built from this branch (MongoDB, an agent whose gated `POST` tool sends `Bearer {context.userToken}`): the resume that re-supplies the token now runs the approved call with it, where 6.6.0 answered 400; re-supplying a key that was not secret in the paused turn is still a 400 and leaves the pause; a resume without the token is still refused; neither token is stored in the conversation afterwards.

## 📘 docs(integrations): Claude Code plugin with `eddi` and `eddi-admin` skills (2026-10-20)

**Repo:** EDDI (`docs/eddi-claude-skills`)

The MCP server gives an assistant EDDI's tools. It does not tell the assistant which tools to chain, which configuration mistakes deploy cleanly and then fail, or which calls need a person's approval. A new Claude Code plugin covers that, in [`integrations/claude-plugin/`](../../integrations/claude-plugin/README.md), with a marketplace entry at `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (`/plugin marketplace add labsai/EDDI`).

- **`eddi`** (every role) covers connecting and reading auth failures, `discover_agents`, keeping one conversation (`chat_managed` by intent, or passing `conversationId` back to `chat_with_agent`), group discussions (sync and async), approvals bound to the `pauseId` that was read, and the user's own memories.
- **`eddi-admin`** (editor and admin) covers orientation, `setup_agent` / `create_api_agent` into `test`, and the change path: `update_resource` → one `apply_agent_changes` → test → promote. It explains the `compatible` decision, and warns that undeploying with `endConversations` ends users' conversations, and that MCP cannot edit a workflow itself (adding a step is a Manager or REST change). It also covers agent test runs, intents, schedules, channels and groups. The references cover the change workflow, five config gotchas, a symptom-to-cause debugging table, groups, and the actions that need a yes, including the partial-result semantics of the GDPR tools.
- **Design:** the skills split along the MCP role tiers, so an `eddi-user` session never loads authoring tools it would get `FORBIDDEN` on. The skills treat everything EDDI returns as data, never as instructions. They point to the server's own `read_docs` instead of copying documentation, so a skill cannot contradict the running version.
- **No `.mcp.json` in the plugin:** each deployment has its own URL. The Claude Code docs also do not confirm `clientId` / `callbackPort` in a checked-in `.mcp.json`, and without them the client would try dynamic registration, which the shipped realm refuses. The README gives the `claude mcp add --client-id eddi-mcp` command instead.
- **Guard:** `SkillToolReferencesTest` (in `engine.mcp`, reusing `McpToolContractTest`'s tool scan) fails if a skill names an unregistered tool or a camelCase argument no tool takes, if it references a `read_docs` page that is missing or that the Dockerfile deletes from the image, if a SKILL.md frontmatter is incomplete, or if the marketplace entry and the plugin name disagree. Writing the skills against the source caught two stale names. `create_schedule`'s description said `cronExpression`, but the argument is `cron`: the description is fixed in [`McpAdminTools`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpAdminTools.java). And the first draft used the REST parameter `endAllActiveConversations` where `undeploy_agent`'s argument is `endConversations`. The argument check exists because of that one.
- **Docs:** [`mcp-server.md`](../mcp-server.md) gains a "Claude Code plugin" subsection under Quick Start. The AGENTS.md §4.6 docs item now names the skills, because the guard catches renames but not new capabilities.

**Not verified:** the skills have not been run against a live server. Operator prompt unchanged: the only server change is one MCP tool description, which the operator (REST) never sees.

## feat(hitl): opt-in reply and unverified approver on resume (2026-10-20)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/hitl-resume-reply-and-approver`)

### What changed and why

- **Reply from resume.** `POST /agents/{conversationId}/resume` accepts the `say` query parameters (`returnCurrentStepOnly`, `returnDetailed`, `returningFields`). With any of them the request waits for the resumed turn (`AsyncResponse`, `agentTimeoutInSeconds`, 408 on timeout, `Retry-After` as on `say`) and returns it through the same snapshot conversion `say` uses, so secret context stays a placeholder. Without them the answer is the empty 200 it always was. `IConversationService.resumeConversation` gained a six-argument form; the three-argument form delegates with `(false, true, [])`.
- **Approver without authentication.** With no authenticated principal, a body `decidedBy` is recorded as `unverified:<value>` (trimmed, 128 characters, `[A-Za-z0-9._@:+-]` only). It is a claim by the caller, not an identity. An authenticated principal still wins and the body is ignored.
- **Tool-call path test.** `ResumeSecretContextApprovedCallTest` drives the real resume loop with the real httpcall provider and `ApiCallExecutor` resolution (HTTP client recorded): a pinned call using `Bearer {context.userToken}` runs with the real header when the token is re-supplied, is refused as before without it, and is still refused when the URI or a body value changed.

Files: [`RestAgentEngine.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestAgentEngine.java), [`IRestAgentEngine.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/api/IRestAgentEngine.java), [`ConversationHitlService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationHitlService.java), [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md).

### Design decisions

- The endpoint is converted to `AsyncResponse` rather than blocking a worker: it is the same shape and timeout contract as `say`, and the no-flag path resumes the async response immediately.
- Presence of any `say` flag, not a particular value, is what asks for the reply, so `returnCurrentStepOnly=false` returns the whole conversation as it does on `say`.
- **Follow-up fixes.** `unverified:` is now a `ReservedPrincipals` prefix (no token may carry it). The resumed reply runs `recordTurnOutcome` like `say`, so an ERROR turn carries `error` and `Retry-After`. A waiting client is always answered: a resume dropped before it ran (cancelled/ended, or refused by the coordinator) is delivered as `onSkipped` (410 for ENDED, else 409, one mapping shared with `say`), and a resume that throws without rendering is delivered as the ERROR snapshot.
- **A reply is a read.** Resume admits approver-only callers (`eddi-approver`, neither owner nor admin), but a reply returns the conversation, and with `returnCurrentStepOnly=false` or `returnDetailed=true` that can be its whole history. A request carrying any reply flag therefore also needs the owner-or-admin access `say` needs; an approver-only caller asking for a reply gets `403` before the resume runs, so the pause is untouched. Raised by review as a Major authorization finding (CWE-863).
- **The watchdog path too.** A resume the runtime routes to its failure callback (the agent-timeout watchdog abandoned it, or a failure escaped the resume body) now answers a client waiting for the reply with the stored state (`EXECUTION_INTERRUPTED` or `ERROR`, the latter with its `error`), once, guarded by the same one-shot flag. Previously that client waited for its own `408`. On a watchdog abandonment the two usually coincide, since both run on `agentTimeoutInSeconds`; the answer matters for a failure that escapes the body early.

---

## feat(hitl): secret context on resume (2026-10-20)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/hitl-resume-secret-context`)

### What changed and why

A per-turn credential sent as secret context (`secret: true`) is replaced by a placeholder when a turn pauses, so every task after an approval that needed it (an approved HTTP tool call acting as the user) failed: a pinned call was refused as "the request changed after it was approved", an unpinned one failed at execution. `POST /agents/{conversationId}/resume` now accepts an optional `context` map in the same shape as a say request. Only entries marked `secret: true` whose key was a secret in the paused turn are accepted; anything else is a 400 before the pause is consumed. The values replace the placeholder for the resumed turn only and go through the same scrubbing as on `say` (shared `registerSecretContext`, `scrubSecretContextValues`, `TurnAuditBuffer` redaction), so the stored step holds the placeholder again afterwards.

Files: [`HitlDecision.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/lifecycle/model/HitlDecision.java) (write-only `context`), [`ConversationHitlService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationHitlService.java) (pre-CAS validation), [`Conversation.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/internal/Conversation.java) (injection and scrub), [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md).

### Design decisions

- Timeout, Slack, MCP and group resumes pass no context and are unchanged. MCP deliberately does not get it: tool arguments are logged and audited.
- Approval binding is not weakened: headers are fingerprinted redacted, so an identical re-supplied token matches (as does a rotated one in a credential header such as `Authorization`); a different value in the URI, query, body or an unrecognised header changes the fingerprint and the call is refused (fail closed). Documented in `hitl.md`.
