## ✨ feat(llm): `classifier` guardrail type — a safety-classifier model as configuration (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/llm-classifier-guardrail`, stacked on `feat/llm-input-output-guardrails`) — WS4 Phase 4b.

### What changed and why

The 4a guardrails are deterministic heuristics. Phase 4b adds one generic model-backed type, `classifier` (input and output), that asks a safety-classifier model for a verdict, so Granite Guardian, ShieldGemma, a hosted moderation model or a TrustyAI-served detector are **configuration values, not code**. EDDI ships and endorses no model.

- **`ClassifierGuardrail`** (`modules/llm/guardrails/impl`) — a `ContentGuardrail` bean, picked up by `GuardrailService`'s type index like every other. Parameters: `provider` (an EDDI provider type) plus that provider's own model parameters in a task's vocabulary (`baseUrl`, `apiKey` — a `${vault:…}` reference, resolved and grant-checked as for a task — `modelName`/`model`, `customParameters`, `maxTokens`, …), and its own keys `promptTemplate`, `risk`, `unsafeLabel` (default `yes`), `safeLabel`, `labelPattern`, `timeoutMs` (default 3000, cap 60000), `includeRetrievedContext` (output), `inputPricePer1M`/`outputPricePer1M`, and the generic `failMode`.
- **Transport is `ChatModelRegistry.getOrCreate`** (made public), not `SafeHttpClient`: classifier endpoints are in-cluster private addresses, the same trust class as a task's model `baseUrl`; the registry also brings vault/global-variable resolution, the model cache and LLM telemetry. `timeoutMs` becomes the provider `timeout` (a stable cache key), and the call itself runs on a virtual thread bounded by `min(timeoutMs, turn deadline − reserve)` (#996); a turn already out of time does not call the classifier.
- **Prompt:** without `promptTemplate` the text goes as the model's native conversation (input: the user message; output: question + answer as an assistant message), which is what Granite Guardian on vLLM and ShieldGemma on Ollama expect — their chat templates build the classification prompt. With `promptTemplate`, the template is rendered by the restricted Qute engine with the screened text as **data** only (`{input}`, `{output}`, `{risk}`, `{context}`) — never as template source.
- **Verdict:** label = first capture group of the **last** `labelPattern` match (Granite's `<score> yes </score>`, after an optional `<think>` trace), else the first word with punctuation trimmed; compared case-insensitively with `unsafeLabel` / `safeLabel`.
- **Could not decide** → new `GuardrailUndecidedException(reason, costUsd)`: `GuardrailService.evaluate` now returns an internal `Evaluation` and the three failure sites (input, history, output) record the exception's reason (`classifier_unavailable`, `classifier_unparseable`) instead of `guardrail_error`, and add its cost. Everything else about failMode is 4a's single code path (closed by default for `block`/`reprompt`).
- **Cost:** priced with `TokenPricing` from the classifier's token usage into `GuardrailVerdict.costUsd`, which 4a's `accumulateCost` folds into `audit:cost` (WS2's tenant metering reads that key) — including the cost of an unparseable answer.
- **Context:** `GuardrailContext` gains `retrievedContext` (this turn's vector-store + `httpCallRag` context, output phase) and `deadline`; the old six-argument constructor stays. `LlmTask` collects the retrieved context it already appends to the system message and passes both. The HITL resume path has no retrieved context (null).
- **Metric:** timer `eddi.guardrail.classifier.duration{provider, outcome}` (provider tagged only after the registry accepted it, so bounded; outcome `safe|unsafe|unparseable|unavailable|timeout`); `eddi.guardrail.triggered` unchanged. Both now have panels on the Full Metrics dashboard — 4a's counter had none, which `MetricsDashboardCoverageTest` flagged.
- **Docs:** [langchain.md](../langchain.md#the-classifier-type-a-safety-classifier-model) (parameters, template safety, could-not-decide, cost/latency, worked examples for Granite Guardian 3.3 on vLLM and ShieldGemma on Ollama), [security.md](../security.md) (the failMode availability trade-off, transport trust), [metrics.md](../metrics.md), [audit-ledger.md](../audit-ledger.md). Operator prompt explains configuring a classifier (it never writes the vault reference itself); revision stays 17 (main is at 16), fingerprint updated. Manager `GuardrailConfig` doc comment lists the type.

### Design decisions

- **`screensHistory()` is false**: a model call per earlier history message on every turn would multiply the cost by the window size; each message was classified on its own turn.
- **`promptTemplate` optional** (the plan had it required): the two documented models expect the raw conversation and build their own prompt; a wrapper prompt would be classified as content.
- **`labelPattern` added** (not in the plan): Granite Guardian answers inside a `<score>` tag, which first-token matching cannot read. It is author config, compiled through 4a's bounded `PatternCache`.
- **Safe is a positive match, never a default** (review follow-up). An answer is safe only when it matches `safeLabel`; a refusal, an HTML error body, `maybe` or a truncated reply is undecided and follows `failMode` (closed by default). `safeLabel` defaults to `no` when `unsafeLabel` is left at `yes`; a custom `unsafeLabel` without `safeLabel` fails the deploy — `LlmTask.configure` now asks each guardrail type to validate its parameters (`ContentGuardrail.validate`, default: nothing to check; unknown types still only WARN at run time). A missing `provider` is refused the same way.
- **`labelPattern` runs over the last 4 KB of the answer** (the first word over the first 4 KB), so an author regex never scans an unexpectedly huge reply.
- **Llama Guard:** not given a worked example. Meta's Llama 3.2/3.3/4 acceptable-use policies withhold the licence rights for their multimodal models from EU-domiciled companies, which covers Llama Guard 4 and Llama Guard 3 Vision; the text-only Llama Guard 3 is mentioned with that caveat, and ShieldGemma is the Ollama example. EDDI takes no position on any model's licence.
- **Provider error messages are never logged or propagated** (they can echo the screened text); only the exception class.

### Manager

- The guardrails editor from the merged Manager PR offers `classifier` again on both sides (`GUARDRAIL_TYPES`, phases, no-rewrite set and type text in `guardrails.ts`); its settings panel and warnings were already in place. The editor test that asserted it was not offered now asserts it is. `operator-revision.json` is bumped for the operator prompt change.

### Not done

- The Granite Guardian `chat_template_kwargs` example is per the model card and vLLM's request extension; it was not run against a live vLLM here.

---

## feat(llm): generic `openai-compatible` provider type for self-hosted servers (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/llm-openai-compatible-provider`; backend, Manager, docs, Platform Operator)

### What changed and why

Production on-prem inference runs on vLLM, llama-server, TGI, KServe or LiteLLM. EDDI could
reach them only as `type: openai` + `baseUrl`, and so judged them as OpenAI: JSON mode was sent
alongside tools (a 400 on servers that do not support the combination), vision was decided by
OpenAI model names (a self-hosted Qwen-VL was text-only), and telemetry reported the traffic as
`OPEN_AI`. The named-provider catalog cannot describe these servers by design: it requires a
fixed `https://` endpoint and a fixed default model.

- **New LLM type `openai-compatible`** (`OpenAiCompatibleEndpoint`,
  `SelfHostedOpenAiLanguageModelBuilder`, registered in `LlmModule` and reserved in
  `BUILT_IN_TYPES` so no catalog row can take the id). It runs on the OpenAI builder.
  `baseUrl` and `modelName` are required; `http://` is accepted and the cloud-metadata block
  stays. `apiKey` is optional: verified against langchain4j 1.22.0's `DefaultOpenAiClient`, a
  null key adds no `Authorization` header, and a blank key is now removed before it could
  become `Bearer `.
- **Per-endpoint capability flags**, in the catalog's own vocabulary
  (`OpenAiCompatibleProvider.Capabilities`): `jsonMode`, `jsonModeWithTools`, `vision`, all
  off by default. `JsonResponseFormatPolicy` gained an `endpoint` component, filled by a new
  `of(..., parameters)` overload at all three call sites: `LlmTask` live, `LlmTask` HITL resume,
  and `CascadingModelExecutor`, which passes the **step's** merged parameters.
  `ModelCapabilityService.visionOverride` places the endpoint's `vision=true` below the per-task
  `multimodal.vision` override and above the deployment override; the built-in default for the
  type is off.
- **Strict literal flags, lenient templated ones.** A literal flag that is neither `true` nor
  `false` fails the deploy (`OpenAiCompatibleConfigValidator`, run from `LlmTask.configure` for
  the task, each cascade step and the judge) with a message naming the parameter; so does a
  missing `baseUrl`/`modelName`. LLM parameters are rendered by Qute before the request is built,
  but `${vars:...}` is otherwise only resolved inside `ChatModelRegistry`, so the call sites
  resolve global variables in the three flags first (`OpenAiCompatibleEndpoint.withResolvedFlags`).
  A templated flag that still is not a boolean counts as `false` with one WARN per distinct value
  (bounded): it never fails the build or the turn. `customParameters` must parse to a JSON object for this type (the OpenAI builder
  only warns and drops it).
- **Telemetry.** `serverName` (default `openai-compatible`) is reported as
  `gen_ai.provider.name` and the `provider` meter tag in place of langchain4j's `OPEN_AI`,
  through a provider-name override on `LlmTelemetryListener.forModelType` and the observable
  model wrappers. It is bounded: lower-cased, 1-32 characters of `[a-z0-9._-]`, else the
  default.
- **Model cache.** The three capability flags are left out of `ChatModelRegistry`'s cache key
  for this type (they shape requests, not the model instance); `serverName` stays in, because
  the telemetry wrapper bakes it in.
- **Parameter catalog.** `model-parameters.json` has an `openai-compatible` row that inherits
  OpenAI's parameters without their model rules and adds `serverName` and the three flags;
  the Manager mirror and [`docs/llm-parameters.md`](../llm-parameters.md) are regenerated. Its inherited `responseFormat` is overridden with a "do not use" note pointing at
  `convertToObject` / `jsonResponseFormat` and `jsonMode`, since the OpenAI builder bakes it into the cached model.
- **Setup.** `AgentSetupService` treats the type as keyless and requires `model` and
  `baseUrl`/`llmBaseUrl` up front instead of injecting the SaaS default model; the MCP
  `setup_agent` / `create_api_agent` descriptions name it.
- **Manager.** "Self-hosted (OpenAI-compatible)" in the local group of the provider picker
  (agent wizard, operator activation, LLM editor, cascade steps, summary provider): required
  model and base URL with examples, an optional API key, and a note that the capability flags
  are switched on under Model settings, where they render from the catalog with a one-line
  explanation each. Pickers with no base URL field do not offer it: the workforce team builder,
  the group wizard's members and moderator, the cascade judge and a group's summarizer. The
  rolling-summary provider offers it only when the task itself is `openai-compatible`, because
  the summarizer then inherits the task's `baseUrl` and key.
  7 i18n keys in all 11 locales.
- **Docs and Operator.** A "Self-hosted OpenAI-compatible servers" section with a migration
  note in [`docs/langchain.md`](../langchain.md), the provider matrix row, and notes in
  [`docs/metrics.md`](../metrics.md) and [`docs/model-cascade.md`](../model-cascade.md). The
  Platform Operator prompt describes the type; `operator-revision.json` is bumped to 24 (one above main).

### Design decisions

- **`customParameters` is the extra-body mechanism**, not a new `extraBody` key: it already is
  a JSON object merged into the request body with the user winning, the Manager already renders
  it, and a second key with the same meaning would need a precedence rule. It is made strict
  for this type only, so `type: openai` behaviour does not change.
- **No `jsonSchema` flag yet.** Native `json_schema` support differs between servers and
  versions, and the plan ties it to the structured-output work (D5); schemaless JSON is sent
  when `jsonMode` allows it.
- **`vision=false` defers rather than vetoes**: absent or `false` both fall through to the
  deployment override and then to the off default, so an operator's
  `eddi.multimodal.openai-compatible.vision=on` still applies to endpoints that did not set it.
- **`type: openai` is untouched**, pinned by a regression test of its JSON-policy rows, its
  native schema, its vision decisions and an old `type: openai` + `baseUrl` config.

### Not done

- Model discovery via `GET /v1/models` and the deployment-level provider allow-list (C3) are out
  of scope.
- `CreateSubAgentTool` has no base URL parameter, so it refuses this type up front with a
  message telling the model to pick another provider or have an administrator create the agent.

## 🔒 fix(deploy): runtime and deployment findings of the 2026-10-17 security review (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/security-review-runtime`)

### What changed and why

The runtime and deployment workstream of the 2026-10-17 security review — M6, M7,
L7, L8 and the small L12/L13 items. One commit per finding.

- **M6 — the authenticated PostgreSQL install could not start, and two stacks had no
  bind-guard.** `docker-compose.auth.yml` overrides `bind-guard`, which only
  `docker-compose.yml` declared, so `-f docker-compose.postgres-only.yml -f
  docker-compose.auth.yml` — exactly what `install.sh --db=postgres --with-auth` and
  `install.ps1` compose — failed with "service bind-guard has neither an image nor a
  build context". Separately `docker-compose.postgres-only.yml` and
  `docker-compose.cluster.yml` publish on `${EDDI_BIND:-127.0.0.1}` with the three
  `*_ALLOW_UNAUTHENTICATED` defaults `true` and no guard, so `EDDI_BIND=0.0.0.0`
  published an open EDDI, MCP server and vault there. Both stacks now carry the same
  guard (on the database image each already pulls; in the cluster the load balancer
  waits for it). Design: the guard stays in every base rather than moving to an
  overlay — a base used on its own must still refuse — and `ComposeStackTest` makes
  the duplication safe: the copies must be byte-identical in script and inputs, every
  stack that publishes on `EDDI_BIND` must have one, every combination the installers
  can assemble (base × `--local` × `--with-auth` × `--with-monitoring`, read back out
  of both installers) and every multi-file command README/`docs/` print is checked
  structurally (every service and `depends_on` target resolves) and, where a Docker CLI
  exists, rendered with `docker compose config`. That sweep also found
  `docs/mcp-server.md` telling users to run `docker compose -f docker-compose.auth.yml
  down -v` on its own, which compose refuses; fixed.
  Files: [`docker-compose.postgres-only.yml`](../../docker-compose.postgres-only.yml),
  [`docker-compose.cluster.yml`](../../docker-compose.cluster.yml),
  [`docker-compose.auth.yml`](../../docker-compose.auth.yml),
  [`ComposeStackTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/ComposeStackTest.java),
  [`docker.md`](../docker.md), [`mcp-server.md`](../mcp-server.md)

- **M7 — the unauthenticated quick start had no `Host`/`Origin` check.** Loopback binding
  stops the network, not the browser: a DNS-rebinding page (`Host: evil.example` resolved to
  `127.0.0.1`) was same-origin with the whole API, MCP and `/secretstore`, and without
  rebinding a page could still send blind CORS "simple" `POST`s. The new
  `UnauthenticatedRequestGuard` (a Vert.x route filter registered like `HttpMethodGuard`,
  and only while `authorization.enabled=false`) answers **421** to a `Host` that is not
  `localhost`/`*.localhost`, an IP literal or an entry of the new
  `eddi.security.unauthenticated.allowed-hosts`, and **403** to a
  `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` whose `Origin` is neither the request's own nor in
  `quarkus.http.cors.origins` (`null` included) or, without an `Origin`, whose
  `Sec-Fetch-Site` is `cross-site`/`same-site`. `/q/health` is exempt; one `[SECURITY]` WARN
  at boot states the policy. Design decision, deviating from the review's "loopback only":
  **IP literals are always answered**. Rebinding needs a DNS name the attacker controls, so an
  address cannot be its product, and kubelet probes, Prometheus scrapes (pod IPs) and
  `eddi.self.base-url` (`http://127.0.0.1:…` or a pod IP) address EDDI that way; the
  cross-site check still covers a page that posts to an address. The raw `Host` header is
  read, never a forwarded one — a rebound page is same-origin and may set
  `X-Forwarded-Host`. The compose stacks default the allow-list to `eddi` (Prometheus and
  Open WebUI reach EDDI by service name), the kustomize base and quickstart to the Service's
  in-cluster names, and the Helm chart (when OIDC is off) to the Service names plus any
  acknowledged ingress host. Chart `2.8.0` → `2.9.0`.
  Files: [`UnauthenticatedRequestGuard.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/UnauthenticatedRequestGuard.java),
  [`UnauthenticatedRequestGuardTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/UnauthenticatedRequestGuardTest.java),
  [`security.md`](../security.md#running-without-authentication),
  [`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md)

- **L7 — runtime defaults.** Done:
  - **HSTS.** The GCP provisioner's nginx sends `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000`
    on both https servers (no `includeSubDomains` — sslip.io names are not ours), and EDDI
    gains an opt-in `HstsHeaderFilter` (`eddi.security.hsts.enabled`, default `false`;
    `eddi.security.hsts.max-age`). The Helm chart sets it when `ingress.enabled` and
    `ingress.tls` are both set.
  - **`/q/health` detail.** The cluster readiness check reports its details (mode, NATS
    state, node id, member count, drain state, `degradedSince`) only with the new
    `eddi.health.verbose=true`; by default the public endpoint carries the status alone.
    `GET /administration/cluster/overview` still has everything, authenticated. (Gated in
    `ClusterHealthCheck` itself, so the readiness data is gone, not merely moved behind a
    path.) `agentsInErrorCount` stays — it is a count, documented as deliberately public.
  - **Keycloak realm** (all three copies, kept in step): `passwordPolicy` (`length(12) and
    maxLength(128) and notUsername and notEmail and passwordHistory(3)` — the installers'
    generated one-time passwords are 32 characters), login and admin events on (30 days,
    admin event bodies off), `eddi-mcp` with `consentRequired: true` and
    `fullScopeAllowed: false` plus a `scopeMappings` entry for exactly the `eddi-*` roles,
    seeded users `emailVerified: false`. Verified by importing the realm into
    `quay.io/keycloak/keycloak:26.8.0` and generating an example `eddi-mcp` access token
    (roles and `aud` still present). Realm import is one-shot: existing realms keep their
    old settings (documented in security.md).
  - **GCP nginx** returns 404 for `/realms/master/` (the provisioner reaches it on
    localhost:8180, not through nginx).
  - **Kubernetes.** The production NetworkPolicy's ingress-controller rule is pinned to the
    `ingress-nginx` namespace by `kubernetes.io/metadata.name` instead of admitting a
    controller-labelled pod from any namespace. MongoDB, PostgreSQL and Keycloak pods (Helm,
    kustomize overlays, quickstart) run as their image's own uid from the start
    (Mongo 999, Postgres alpine 70 — Helm `postgres.runAsUser` — Keycloak 1000) with
    `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, `capabilities.drop: [ALL]` and the RuntimeDefault
    seccomp profile; both databases were started that way in Docker
    (`--user … --cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges`) to confirm their
    entrypoints still initialise.
  - `quarkus.swagger-ui.oauth2-redirect-url` is no longer pinned to
    `http://localhost:7070`; Swagger UI derives it from the page's origin.
  - `docker-compose.yml`'s MongoDB healthcheck no longer carries the root password on its
    command line (`docker inspect`, host process list): `ping` needs no authentication.

  Deliberately **not** done here: Keycloak `start-dev` and the `admin/admin` compose fallback
  (the installers and the GCP provisioner already generate one; a production Keycloak mode
  needs a database and hostname design of its own), EDDI connecting to MongoDB as root,
  plaintext service-to-service traffic, NATS without auth and the unauthenticated MongoDB in
  the kustomize quickstart, and `quarkus.log.console.level=DEBUG` (the handler level only;
  categories stay at INFO).
  Files: [`HstsHeaderFilter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/HstsHeaderFilter.java),
  [`ClusterHealthCheck.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterHealthCheck.java),
  [`eddi-realm.json`](../../keycloak/eddi-realm.json),
  [`provision-vm.sh`](../../gcp/provision-vm.sh),
  [`network-policy.yaml`](../../k8s/overlays/production/network-policy.yaml)

- **L8 — installer integrity (the minimal version).** The release job in `ci.yml` now
  checks out the tag, hashes every file the installers download (`docker-compose*.yml`, the
  realm, the login theme, `docs/monitoring/`) into `SHA256SUMS` and attaches it to the
  GitHub release. `install.sh` and `install.ps1` fetch that asset when `EDDI_BRANCH` is a
  release tag and refuse a downloaded file whose hash differs (the file is deleted); with no
  asset — `main`, a branch, every release before this one — they warn that only TLS vouches
  for the files and carry on, so existing installs do not break. Exercised with stubbed
  downloads for both scripts (accept, unlisted → warn, mismatch → refuse and delete,
  non-release ref → warn). `gcp/provision-vm.sh` defaults `--eddi-branch` to the
  `--eddi-version` tag or the newest stable release (GitHub API), falling back to `main`
  with a warning. `postgres:16-alpine` is pinned to `postgres:16.15-alpine` in the compose
  stack (database and bind-guard), the kustomize overlay and the Helm values. **Not done:**
  the installers cannot verify themselves (`curl | bash` from `main` stays TLS-only), the
  CLI's `eddi update` refresh path does not verify, there is no cosign signature on the
  asset, compose still defaults to `labsai/eddi:latest`, and `mcp-sidecar/Dockerfile`'s
  unpinned `nodejs npm` is untouched.
  Files: [`install.sh`](../../install.sh), [`install.ps1`](../../install.ps1),
  [`ci.yml`](../../.github/workflows/ci.yml), [`security.md`](../security.md#installer-downloads)
  

- **L12/L13 — small items.** `quarkus.http.cors.exposed-headers` now exposes
  `Mcp-Session-Id` and `Retry-After` next to `Location`, so a browser-based MCP client on an
  allowed origin can read its session id and a 429/503's back-off. The `7443` mapping the
  compose stacks publish is documented rather than removed: it is Quarkus' HTTPS port,
  opened only once a certificate is configured, and removing it would have touched every
  installer's port resolution for no security gain (nothing listens). Dependabot #716 was
  *not* stale, contrary to the review: it bumps the `eclipse-temurin:25-jre` digest in
  `src/main/docker/Dockerfile.demo` (the Open WebUI demo image), which still uses that base
  — nothing changed here.
  Files: [`application.properties`](../../src/main/resources/application.properties),
  [`docker-compose.yml`](../../docker-compose.yml), [`docker.md`](../docker.md)

- **M7 follow-up (live dev-mode test).** `host.docker.internal`,
  `gateway.docker.internal` and `host.containers.internal` are now answered built in — that
  is how a container reaches an EDDI on the host, the container runtime's resolver answers
  them, and `.internal` is a reserved TLD public DNS never delegates. Three unexpected
  `400`s from the live test (`Host: eddi.localhost:7070`, `Host: localhost.`, and
  `/q/health/live` with `Host: evil.example`) were traced to Quarkus 3.40's own
  `HostValidationFilter`, not this guard: it is switched on in dev and test mode because the
  server binds `localhost`, accepts only `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`[::1]` and ends anything
  else with an empty `400` (confirmed from its bytecode, and by the guard answering
  `evil.example` with its own `421` on a non-exempt path of the same instance). The released
  6.5.0 image (binds `0.0.0.0`, filter off) answers all three with `200`. The guard's own
  decisions for the three are pinned in `UnauthenticatedRequestGuardTest`, and
  `docs/security.md` explains the dev-mode `400`.

### Design decisions

- `bind-guard` is duplicated into every base stack rather than moved to an overlay, and a
  test pins the copies together — moving it out would have left `docker compose up` on a
  base alone unguarded.
- The M7 guard answers any IP-literal `Host` (see above) and reads only the raw `Host`
  header; `*` in the allow-list switches the Host check off, logged at boot.
- `/q/health` detail is gated in the health check (`eddi.health.verbose`) instead of
  narrowing the public permit, because the sensitive data is in `/q/health/ready` itself.
- Installer checksum verification is warn-only when no `SHA256SUMS` exists, so every
  release before this one keeps installing.

### What's next

- The Platform Operator (`ui/manager/src/lib/operator/system-prompt.ts`, WS-F's area) does
  not yet know about `eddi.security.unauthenticated.allowed-hosts`, `eddi.health.verbose` or
  `eddi.security.hsts.enabled`; an admin asking "why 421?" will not get an answer from it.
- L7 and L8 items listed as not done above.

- **The review itself is now in the repository.** `planning/security-review-2026-10-17.md` is the report every `security review 2026-10-17, <id>` reference in code and fragments points to, with a status line, the live-probe results, what was verified clean and the remediation table; `docs/security-review.md` gains an entry for the October reviews.

## 🔒 fix(security): editors no longer read plaintext credentials in any config store (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/security-review-secrets-redaction`)

### What changed and why

M1 of the 2026-10-17 security review. The 2026-10-15 fix (L12) redacted plaintext
keys for non-admins in `tasks[].parameters` of LLM configurations only; editors still
read them verbatim in `a2aAgents[].apiKey`, `modelCascade` step and judge parameters,
the MCP-calls `apiKey`, a Slack integration's `botToken`/`signingSecret` (enough to
forge `/integrations/slack/*` events), RAG `embeddingParameters`/`storeParameters`,
and literal `Authorization` headers and keys in an httpcall's `request.path` and
`request.body` — current and old (`?version=`) versions alike.

- **One shared read-redactor**, `ConfigSecretRedactor` (`secrets.sanitize`), walks the
  serialized document: credential-named fields are replaced whole, header and
  query-parameter maps are judged by header/parameter name, URLs lose their userinfo
  password and credential-named query values, and `body` fields go through
  `SecretRedactionFilter`. References (`${vault:}`, `${vars:}`, `${connection:}`,
  `${caller:}`) and templates pass unchanged, also beside a scheme word
  (`Bearer ${vault:k}`). `token` matches only singular, so `maxTokens` and
  `thinkingBudgetTokens` are untouched; `httpCalls[].parameters` (tool parameter
  descriptions) and `tasks[].toolCacheScopes` (keyed by tool name) are skipped.
- `RestLlmStore`, `RestMcpCallsStore`, `RestChannelIntegrationStore`, `RestRagStore`
  and `RestApiCallsStore` apply it for every caller who is not `eddi-admin`. MCP
  `read_resource`/`read_channel_integration` reach the same REST stores through the
  loopback client, which forwards the caller, so they are covered too.
- `RestLlmStore`'s write-time warning now uses the same detection, so it warns about
  exactly what an editor cannot read back.
- `RestRagIngestion` reads the knowledge base through the new
  `RestRagStore.readUnredacted` (same access check, no redaction): ingestion
  builds the embedding model from that document and must not send the marker.

**Design decision:** no merge-back of the stored literal when an editor saves a
redacted document — the marker is saved and fails at the provider. Merging it back
would let an editor move the marker next to a host of their choosing (H1 with extra
steps).

Upgrade and sync read their targets unredacted, and the previews an editor sees
are redacted at the REST boundary — see the last entry below.

**Files:** [`ConfigSecretRedactor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/sanitize/ConfigSecretRedactor.java),
[`RestLlmStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/llm/rest/RestLlmStore.java),
[`RestMcpCallsStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/mcpcalls/rest/RestMcpCallsStore.java),
[`RestChannelIntegrationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/channels/rest/RestChannelIntegrationStore.java),
[`RestRagStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/rest/RestRagStore.java),
[`RestRagIngestion.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/rest/RestRagIngestion.java),
[`RestApiCallsStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/apicalls/rest/RestApiCallsStore.java),
[`security.md`](../security.md#editors-never-read-a-plaintext-credential-back),
[`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md#configuration-read-redaction)

**Tests:** `ConfigSecretRedactorTest` (rules, idempotence, a linear-time check on
adversarial braces), one `*ReadRedactionTest` per store; each store test was
mutation-checked by removing the redaction and watching it fail.

## 🔒 fix(security): resetting a tenant's vault requires confirm=true (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/security-review-secrets-redaction`)

L11 of the 2026-10-17 security review. `POST /secretstore/secrets/{tenantId}/reset`
deletes every secret and DEK of a tenant and had no confirmation, unlike
`adopt-master-key`. It now requires `?confirm=true`; without it the answer is a `400`
with `{"error": "confirm=true is required", "action": …}` and nothing is deleted. The
Manager's reset dialog (which already makes the admin type the tenant id) sends the
flag, and the `adopt-master-key` response names it.

**Files:** [`IRestSecretStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/rest/IRestSecretStore.java),
[`RestSecretStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/rest/RestSecretStore.java),
[`secrets.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/api/secrets.ts),
[`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md#lost-master-key)

## 🔒 fix(security): name the vault secrets that may still be sent anywhere (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/security-review-secrets-redaction`)

The residual of H1 in the 2026-10-17 security review: PR #1079 makes the setup wizard
set `allowedHosts` on every key it vaults from now on, but every secret vaulted before
it keeps an open destination, so an editor can still exfiltrate it by pointing a copy of
the LLM configuration at their own host.

- New `UnrestrictedSecretsCheck` (next to `VaultStartupBanner`): when the vault is
  enabled it scans, on a virtual thread after boot, the current version of every LLM,
  MCP-calls, RAG, httpcalls and channel configuration for `${vault:…}` references, reads
  each referenced secret's metadata, and logs **one** `[SECURITY]` WARN naming every
  secret without `allowedHosts` (`tenant/key`), the configurations that reference it,
  and the `PUT /secretstore/secrets/{tenantId}/{keyName}/grant` call that narrows it.
- `GET /secretstore/secrets/{tenantId}` (admin-only) now returns `SecretListing` entries:
  the same metadata fields as before plus `unrestrictedDestination` (live: `allowedHosts`
  empty) and `referencedBy` (from the last complete scan; absent before one).
- The scan says when it could not read every configuration: one `[SECURITY]` WARN, and
  `referencedBy` stays absent rather than claiming that nothing references a secret the
  unread configuration may use.

**Design decision:** no automatic migration. Only the admin knows which host a key
belongs to; a wrong guess would break a working agent. `AgentSetupService` and
`VaultSecretProvider` are untouched (PR #1079 changes them).

**Not covered:** references reached only through `${vars:…}` or `${connection:…}`, and
old configuration versions that are still deployed. No Manager warning state yet (UI).

**Files:** [`UnrestrictedSecretsCheck.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/UnrestrictedSecretsCheck.java),
[`SecretListing.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/rest/SecretListing.java),
[`RestSecretStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/rest/RestSecretStore.java),
[`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md#destination-allow-list-allowedhosts)

---

## 🔒 fix(security): the free-text redaction filter knows more provider key shapes (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/security-review-secrets-redaction`)

L6 of the 2026-10-17 security review. `SecretRedactionFilter` — which backs logs, dead
letters, the audit scrub, HITL approval cards and now the config read-redaction —
recognised only `sk-`, `sk-ant-`, `Bearer` and named fields. Patterns **added** (no
existing rule changed): Google `AIza…`, xAI `xai-…`, Groq `gsk_…`, GitHub `gh[pousr]_…`
and `github_pat_…`, Slack `xox[baprse]-…`, AWS `AKIA…`/`ASIA…` (exactly 16, not inside
a longer run), bare JWTs, the credential after `Authorization: Basic|Token|Digest`,
`…Subscription-Key:` headers, and the userinfo password of `mongodb(+srv)`, `postgres(ql)`,
`mysql`, `redis(s)` and `amqp(s)` connection strings. (`api-key:`/`x-api-key:` were
already matched by the API-key name rule.)

Every pattern is a literal prefix plus one possessive or bounded character class that
admits no quote, backslash or brace, so a match cannot cross a JSON string or eat a
`${vault:…}` reference. The JWT rule is the one that can fail after consuming a long run,
so a lookbehind admits a start only where a token run begins — without it `eyJ` repeated
60,000 times is quadratic (the mutation check timed out at 30 s).

The connection-string rule had a 256-character ceiling on the user and on the password,
and stopped at the first `@`, so a longer password — or a malformed one carrying a raw
`@` (`u:abc@tail:pw@host`) — went through in part. The password is now walked by hand to
the last `@` of the authority, in one pass. The labelled scheme rule lost its six-character
floor (`Authorization: Basic YTpi` is `a:b`), and a quote escaped inside a JSON-carried
Digest value no longer ends that credential early. Two gaps found in review: a `Digest` header lost only `username=` — the scheme rule
took one unquoted token and left `response=`, the credential proper, legible — and now
loses its whole directive list, walked by hand rather than by a pattern, since a
quantified group recurses once per directive; and a bare `Basic <base64>` with no
header name in front is redacted when the token decodes to `user:password`, so
`Basic configuration` stays prose.

**Files:** [`SecretRedactionFilter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/sanitize/SecretRedactionFilter.java),
[`SecretRedactionFilterProviderShapesTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/sanitize/SecretRedactionFilterProviderShapesTest.java),
[`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md#log-redaction)

---

## 🔒 fix(security): legacy no-AAD vault rows are re-sealed bound on first read (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/security-review-secrets-redaction`)

L10 of the 2026-10-17 security review. `VaultSecretProvider.decryptSecretWithFallback`
kept a permanent no-AAD fallback for rows written before row binding, so with database
write access the ciphertexts of two such rows could be swapped and each still decrypted.
Only a DEK rotation retired the fallback, and a deployment that never rotates kept it
forever.

- `resolve()` now notices when a row opened only through the legacy path and re-seals
  it bound (`tenantId|keyName|dekId` AAD) under the **same** DEK generation, through the
  new `ISecretPersistence.resealSecretInPlace` (Mongo and Postgres). It rewrites only the
  ciphertext and IV — not `dekId`, not `lastRotatedAt` — and is guarded on the **IV** the
  row was read with: a dekId guard cannot see a concurrent `store` under the same
  generation and would write the old value back over the new one. A lost guard, a
  failure or an implementation without the method (the interface default writes nothing)
  leaves the row legacy and readable; the read never fails because of it — the write-back
  catches every `RuntimeException`, so a driver or pool error a store did not wrap cannot
  fail it either. Logged once per key per node.
- `openSealedData` (OAuth grants and other participant-owned sealed data) cannot write
  back — the provider does not own those rows — so it logs once per tenant that
  `POST /secretstore/secrets/{tenantId}/rotate-dek` re-seals them bound.

**Files:** [`VaultSecretProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/impl/VaultSecretProvider.java),
[`ISecretPersistence.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/persistence/ISecretPersistence.java),
[`MongoSecretPersistence.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/persistence/MongoSecretPersistence.java),
[`PostgresSecretPersistence.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/persistence/PostgresSecretPersistence.java),
[`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md#encryption)

**Tests:** `VaultLegacySealingRetirementTest` (re-seal on read, swap refused afterwards,
a store racing the write-back wins, a failing write-back does not fail the read) and
guard tests in the Mongo and Postgres persistence unit tests; mutation-checked.

## 🔒 fix(security): the read-redaction marker is never stored; upgrade and sync read targets unredacted (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/security-review-secrets-redaction`)

Follow-up to the M1 read-redaction above, so it can never turn into stored corruption.

- **Writes refuse the marker.** New `RedactionMarkerGuard` (`configs/rest`): every create,
  update, JSON Patch and duplicate of the LLM, MCP-calls, channel, RAG and httpcalls
  stores, `RestImportService.createResourceDirect` and `UpgradeExecutor.createExtension`
  answer `400 {"error": "bad_request", "message": …, "fields": […]}` when any string in the
  document contains `<redacted: `, for administrators too. The import and upgrade paths
  create every resource type through one method, so there the check is scoped to those
  five document types (`requireNoneIfRedactedType`): a workflow or output set that merely
  mentions the marker text is content and imports. Before this, an editor who
  opened a configuration in the Manager and saved it replaced the stored key with the
  marker, and the agent broke at its next deploy.
- **One recognisable marker.** The in-string marker `ConfigSecretRedactor` writes into
  URLs, headers and bodies is now `<redacted: credential>` instead of the filter's
  generic `<REDACTED>`, which a prompt or output template may legitimately contain. Both
  markers share the `<redacted: ` prefix the guard looks for.
- **Unredacted internal reads.** `RestLlmStore`, `RestMcpCallsStore`, `RestApiCallsStore`
  and `RestRagStore` expose `readUnredacted` (same access check; `readRagForIngestion`
  became `readUnredacted`). `StructuralMatcher` reads upgrade/sync targets through it
  instead of the loopback REST client, so an editor-run upgrade neither diffs against the
  marker nor writes it: the export's `${vault:REDACTED}` placeholder is replaced by the
  target's own key.
- A sync **source** read from a peer through a non-admin credential carries the peer's
  marker; such resources are now refused rather than stored.
- **Previews are redacted for editors.** The matcher's unredacted target content is
  also what the upgrade and sync previews return, and those endpoints admit
  `eddi-editor`, so `RestImportService` redacts `sourceContent` and `targetContent`
  for every caller who is not `eddi-admin` (`PreviewRedaction`, with the stores'
  redactor and skipped paths) before a preview leaves the server. The upgrade itself
  still writes from the unredacted read. (Review finding on the PR.)

**Tests:** a `*RedactionMarkerWriteTest` per store, a marker case in
`RestImportServiceCallerTokenTest`, `EditorUpgradeKeepsTargetCredentialTest` (an
editor-run upgrade of an agent whose LLM config holds a plaintext key keeps it and is
not refused), `PreviewRedactionTest` and `RestImportServicePreviewRedactionTest`; each
mutation-checked.

**Files:** [`RedactionMarkerGuard.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rest/RedactionMarkerGuard.java),
[`ConfigSecretRedactor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/sanitize/ConfigSecretRedactor.java),
[`PreviewRedaction.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/PreviewRedaction.java),
[`StructuralMatcher.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/StructuralMatcher.java),
[`UpgradeExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/UpgradeExecutor.java),
[`RestImportService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/RestImportService.java),
[`security.md`](../security.md#editors-never-read-a-plaintext-credential-back)

## 🔒 fix(manager,chat): UI findings from the 2026-10-17 security review (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/security-review-ui`)

### What changed and why

The UI half of the 2026-10-17 review (L1, L5, and the Manager side of M5). The backend halves are separate branches.

- **L1, Chat `?apiServer=`.** `http.ts` builds request URLs by concatenating the base and the path, and the allow-list let `?` and `#` through, so `?apiServer=%2Fadministration%2Fcluster%2Fcaches%2Fresync%3F` made the widget's start call a state-changing request to another same-origin path, carrying the page's credentials. `sanitizeApiServer` now parses the value against the page origin and accepts it only when the origin is unchanged, `search` and `hash` are empty, the pathname is exactly the input (so `..` and `.` segments are refused), and it has no `%`. Documented in [`chat-ui-embedding.md`](../chat-ui-embedding.md).
- **L5a, opt-in agent HTML.** `sanitizeAgentHtml` now forbids `class` (Tailwind utilities could paint a full-viewport fake prompt) and the `svg`, `math`, `image`, `feImage` and `use` elements (render-time fetches), and forces `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"` on every anchor through a hook registered only for the call.
- **L5b, ids in request paths.** New `lib/api/path-segment.ts`: `seg()` encodes an id and refuses `/`, `\`, `..`, whitespace, control characters and empty values; `nameSeg()` encodes a free-form name (connection name, tenant, secret key) and refuses only `.`, `..` and empty. Every id interpolated into a path under `lib/api/` now goes through one of them (about 160 call sites in 32 files). The chat panel drops an unsafe `?agentId=` / `?conversationId=` before issuing `GET /agents/<id>`.
- **L5c, `navigateAway`.** The OAuth link flow navigates only to an `https:` URL, or `http:` for localhost, 127.0.0.1 and [::1], without embedded credentials (`lib/authorization-url.ts`). Anything else is a failed start with the existing "Could not start linking." toast.
- **L5d, pinned Workforce responses.** The `localStorage` key now names the signed-in principal (`workforce-pins-<principal>:<board>`), and sign-out removes every `workforce-pins-*` key.
- **M5, Platform Operator trust.** `platform.operator` is writable by `eddi-editor`. The Manager now opens the operator chat, history and conversation restore only when the agent's descriptor carries `[eddi-platform-operator]` **and** the approval gate verifies on every version (`useOperatorTrust`, `lib/operator/operator-trust.ts`). Otherwise the drawer and the operator page show an "operator could not be verified" panel with *Check again*, and fail closed when a check cannot be completed. Before an **Upgrade** that keeps the stored instructions, or a **Reconfigure** that carries them over, the admin sees a line diff against the default (and the full stored text) and must tick a confirmation. The Manager also does nothing *to* an agent it could not verify (review follow-up on PR #1082): the marker is read at the agent's **current** version from the store, never at `config.version` (that number sits in the same editor-writable variable as the id, so its author chose which descriptor would be inspected); an unverified agent gets no *Check connection*, *Deactivate* or upgrade notice, and a paused one no *Turn back on*; *Reconfigure* provisions the replacement with `retirePredecessor: false`, which leaves the old agent exactly as it was and reports that in the superseded banner; and *Delete operator* becomes *Unregister operator* (`useUnregisterOperator`), which drops `platform.operator` and touches no agent. 21 new strings in all eleven locales.

### Design decisions

- The marker is writable by anyone who can edit the descriptor, so the check is a tripwire, not a signature. The real fix is reserving `platform.*` for `eddi-admin` on the backend (other branch). It is stated in the docs.
- An unverified agent is left alone rather than retired on Reconfigure or deleted on Delete, even though the admin asked: `platform.operator` can name *any* agent, so a retire-and-replace flow would be the one-click way for an editor to have an admin permanently delete someone else's agent and its resources. The admin gets the Agents screen instead, where the agent is shown for what it is.
- `seg()` throws rather than sending a mangled request. Free-form names get the lighter `nameSeg()` because existing tests (and real tenants and secret keys) contain spaces and slashes that `encodeURIComponent` already makes inert.
- No change to `system-prompt.ts` or `tool-scopes.ts`, so `operator-revision.json` is not bumped.

### Not done / follow-up

- The Stryker scope includes `src/lib/operator/**`; the mutation run was not repeated for the two new files there (`operator-trust.ts`, `prompt-diff.ts`).

**Files:** [`ChatWidget.tsx`](../../ui/chat/src/components/ChatWidget.tsx), [`markdown-safe.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/markdown-safe.tsx), [`path-segment.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/api/path-segment.ts), [`authorization-url.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/authorization-url.ts), [`workforce-pins.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/workforce-pins.ts), [`operator-trust.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/operator/operator-trust.ts), [`operator-untrusted.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/components/operator/operator-untrusted.tsx)

## ⚡ fix(ui): clicks that never wait on I/O, and a Chat UI that holds up on phones (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/ui-click-responsiveness`)

Half of a review of both UIs for click latency, every screen size and every
state. Four read-only reviews (click responsiveness, layout/RTL, Manager state
handling, Chat UI) produced about 100 verified findings. This PR holds the
Manager's click-latency fixes and the whole Chat UI; the Manager's layout, RTL
and state fixes are in the companion PR on `fix/manager-layout-a11y`. The
two share no file and build independently. Agent cards here carry the
`reveal-on-touch` class, which that PR defines; until it lands the class is
inert.

### Clicks no longer wait on I/O (Manager)

- **Optimistic updates.** New [`lib/optimistic.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/optimistic.ts)
  (`patchQueries` / `restoreQueries`). The schedule enable toggle flips its row
  at once and rolls back on failure; only the clicked row is disabled, not
  every toggle on the page. Notification mark-read/dismiss update the badge and
  the list immediately, and busy state is per item.
- **Mutations no longer hold their pending state through a refetch.**
  `onSuccess`/`onSettled` arrows that *returned* `invalidateQueries(...)` made
  TanStack wait for the refetch before call-site callbacks ran (closing a
  dialog, navigating). Fixed in `use-cluster`, `use-group-templates`,
  `use-group-workspace` and `use-user-conversations`. The share dialog no longer
  awaits five invalidations one by one before re-enabling its controls.
- **Deploy no longer refetches every agent card.** Deploy and undeploy
  invalidated all of `agentKeys.all`, so every card's status query refired.
  `agentDeploymentQueries(agentIds)` in [`query-keys.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/query-keys.ts)
  matches only the deployed agent's entries and the environment listings, and
  refreshes any sub-key it does not recognise.
- **Filters stop blanking the list.** Search on agents and workflows, the
  cluster leases and dead-letter filters, and the logs instance filter are
  debounced (250–300 ms) and use `placeholderData: keepPreviousData`.
- **Fewer sequential round trips.** The "where is this used" scan after a
  resource save fetches workflows and agents with bounded concurrency (8), and
  fetches each agent once rather than once per matching workflow. Grant dry
  runs run in parallel, and so do the agent editor sheet's post-save
  invalidations.

### Chat UI

- Layout: a long code line or a wide table widened the bubble and scrolled the
  whole transcript sideways (`min-width: 0`, plus a table scroller). Inputs are
  16px so iOS does not zoom on focus. The viewport meta adds `viewport-fit=cover`
  and `interactive-widget=resizes-content`, and the root height is `100dvh`, so
  the composer clears both the home indicator and the keyboard. Very wide
  screens cap line length. Lists, blockquotes and tables use logical properties,
  and messages carry `dir="auto"`.
- Scrolling: pressing Scroll to bottom during a reply turned following off again
  as soon as the smooth scroll began, because its own scroll events read as
  "left the bottom". Now only the reader moving up ends following. The view also
  re-pins for content that grows without a new message (the throttled trailing
  render, images, KaTeX, a shrinking viewport), and the button scrolls the
  transcript rather than the host page.
- Indicators: no more dots under a reply that is already streaming. Undo, redo,
  retry and cancel no longer show the typing dots or a Stop button. Pressing
  Stop then would have cancelled the conversation and raised a false
  "interrupted" banner.
- Focus and input: focus moves to the composer after a quick reply or Stop, and
  back to the New-conversation button when its confirmation is cancelled. Quick
  replies render disabled while a click would be dropped. On touch screens Enter
  inserts a newline. A refused draft regrows to its height. An agent-requested
  field no longer steals focus on page load.
- Undo and redo rebuild messages with position-derived ids, so unchanged
  bubbles keep their keys and are not re-animated and re-parsed. The status
  strings "Thinking…", "Using {tool}…" and the scroll button label are
  translated.

### Team setup, HITL and background polling

- **Group and Workforce wizards set their members up in parallel.**
  `runDeployManyWithGrants` deploys `DEPLOY_MANY_CONCURRENCY` (3) targets at a
  time instead of one after another, and both wizards create new agents three
  at a time. The grant dialog still asks one question at a time: a new request
  used to cancel the open one, which is why the loop was sequential, so the
  per-agent second-chance dialogs now go through `serializeGrantDecisions`.
  Every agent that was created is recorded before a failure is reported,
  because provisioning is not idempotent. The Workforce wizard raises one
  toast for the batch, and each failed row shows its own error.
- **HITL human-input submit is optimistic.** The cached group conversation
  leaves `AWAITING_HUMAN_INPUT` at once and is put back if the submit fails. It
  used to await two refetches, which kept the button pending for an extra round
  trip.
- **The Chat UI no longer polls approval status from a hidden tab.** The next
  read is parked and taken at once when the tab is visible again.

### Review fixes

- Optimistic rollbacks are scoped to their own operation. A failed schedule
  toggle undoes only its row, and a failed notification dismiss undoes only its
  items and its share of the badge, instead of restoring a whole snapshot over a
  later change. Every schedule row with a toggle in flight stays disabled
  (`useMutationState`), not just the latest one.
- The share dialog refreshes the agent's detail and descriptor queries as well
  as the lists. Those keys have their own roots, `agent` and
  `agent-descriptor`, not `agents`.
- Dead-letter paging is disabled while a page is still placeholder data, so a
  second click cannot push the same cursor twice.
- The logs instance box follows back/forward navigation.
- Schedule edits made through the trigger tabs, cron presets and the agent
  picker count as edits, so Escape or a backdrop click no longer discards them.
- The Chat composer takes a pending focus request only once the turn has
  finished, so the phone keyboard does not cover a reply as it arrives.

### Not done (follow-ups)

- A client-side idle watchdog for a stalled chat stream was considered and
  rejected. The conversation stream sends no keep-alives (only the log stream
  does), so a single slow tool or model call is legitimately silent for
  minutes, and a watchdog would cut real turns off as "connection lost". It
  needs a server-side heartbeat first.
- Workforce analytics and the skill registry still fire one request per group or
  skill; both need batch endpoints on the backend.

### Tests

New: [`responsiveness-review.test.tsx`](../../ui/chat/src/components/responsiveness-review.test.tsx)
and a Scroll-to-bottom regression in `ChatWidget.ux.test.tsx` (Chat), and
[`optimistic-mutations.test.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/hooks/__tests__/optimistic-mutations.test.tsx)
(Manager), plus [`deploy-many-concurrency.test.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/hooks/__tests__/deploy-many-concurrency.test.ts)
and a hidden-tab case in `useHitlPolling.test.ts`. Mutation-checked: restoring
the old scroll handler, dropping the dialog queue (in the serializer or in its
use), dropping the HITL cache patch and ignoring visibility each fail a test.

## feat(manager): model picker that always shows the whole catalog, and a refreshed model list (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/manager-model-picker`)

### What changed and why

**The model field no longer hides the catalog.** The agent wizard, the LLM
editor and the operator activation form used a native `<datalist>`, which
filters its options by whatever the input holds. The field is pre-filled with
the provider's default, so clicking it showed one option, the default, and read
as "this provider has one model". The new `ModelCombobox`
(`ui/manager/src/components/shared/model-combobox.tsx`) replaces all three:

- A click, the chevron or ArrowDown opens the whole list with the current value
  ticked and scrolled into view. The provider default carries a `default` badge.
- Typing puts matches under a "Matching …" heading and keeps every other
  suggestion below a divider under "Other models". Matching ignores case and
  separators, so `opus 5.5` finds `claude-opus-5-5`.
- When nothing matches, a note says the typed id is used as typed. A footer
  repeats that any id the provider supports can be typed. The suggestions are
  help, not a whitelist.
- It follows the ARIA combobox pattern (`aria-activedescendant`, grouped
  listbox). Escape closes only the list, not the dialog around it. A read-only
  field gets no list.

The grouping lives in `groupModelSuggestions` in `lib/model-suggestions.ts`.
The 7 new `modelCombobox.*` strings are translated into all 11 locales.

**Catalog refresh.** Every id was checked against the provider's model and
deprecation pages on 2026-10-17.

- **Added:**
  - `claude-haiku-5-5` (Anthropic, Vertex, Bedrock)
  - `gemini-4-argon` (Gemini, Vertex, OpenRouter)
  - The GPT-6 family on Azure and Bedrock
  - `mistral-large-4`
  - `grok-build-0.1`
  - Grok, Gemini and Llama 4 Scout on OCI
  - Gemma 4, Qwen 3.5/3.6, Ministral 3 and gpt-oss on Ollama
  - Popular OpenRouter slugs
- **Removed:**
  - OpenAI: `o3-mini` (shut down 2026-10-23), `gpt-5.4-nano`, the `gpt-5`
    family, and `gpt-5.4-thinking`, which was never an API id
  - Gemini: `gemini-3.7-flash` and `gemini-3.5-flash`, both deprecated on
    AI Studio
  - Vertex: `gemini-2.5-*`, retiring 2026-10-20
  - MiniMax: the M2.7 pair (retiring 2026-11-18). M3.1-Flash-Preview stays,
    because the parameter catalog has rules for it, though MiniMax currently
    offers it only on its subscription plans
  - Bedrock: models now Legacy or past end of life (Llama 3.x/4, MiniMax M2)
  - Azure: deprecated gpt-4o and gpt-4.1
- **Fixed ids that never existed:**
  - Hugging Face: `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4`, `google/gemma-4-assistant` and
    `Qwen/Qwen3.5-7B`
  - Bedrock: `anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6-v1:0`
  - OCI: the `openai/gpt-oss-*` ids, which take a dot after the vendor, not a
    slash
  - Vertex: `claude-haiku-4-5@20251001`
- **Bedrock** now suggests `global.` inference-profile ids. Most current models
  have no in-Region endpoint on `bedrock-runtime`.
- **Defaults:**
  - Gemini and Vertex: `gemini-3.5-flash` → `gemini-3.8-flash`
  - Hugging Face: `Qwen/Qwen3.5-7B`, which does not exist → `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B`
- The named-compatible changes (xAI, MiniMax, OpenRouter) are made in both
  `openai-compatible-providers.json` and its Manager mirror.
- The operator's system prompt lists these suggestions, so the operator
  revision is bumped.

### Not done

`ModelCombobox` is not added to the design-sync mirror (`.design-sync/`).

## ⚡ perf(engine): convert only the current step for a turn's response, drop per-turn INFO logging (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI (`perf/hotpath-and-startup`)

### What changed and why

Part of the throughput work ([`planning/throughput-optimization-plan.md`](../../planning/throughput-optimization-plan.md), WS3b and WS8.5). A JProfiler run at 1,000 concurrent conversations put 9% of running time in Jackson and about 2% in per-turn `Logger.info` calls and `String.format`.

- **Current-step-only conversion (WS3b).** The memory overload of `ConversationMemoryUtilities.convertSimpleConversationMemorySnapshot` converted every step of the conversation and then returned only the last one, which is the default turn response (`returnCurrentStepOnly=true`) and the HITL replies. With `returnCurrentStepOnly` it now builds the snapshot through `convertConversationMemoryForCurrentStep`: same header, outputs, properties and redo cache, and a step list of the same size whose entries are converted only when read. The projection reads the last step and the list's size, so a turn converts one step instead of all of them. Redaction, secret-turn masking, the error field and the undo/redo flags are unchanged: they run in `convertSimpleConversationMemory`, which still does the work. A reader that walks every step still gets correct values, because each step converts on first access; it just gets no saving. Callers in `ConversationService` needed no change.
- **A quadratic loop in `convertConversationMemory`.** `IConversationMemory.getAllSteps()` copies the whole step list on every call, and the conversion called it twice per step. It is now called once. The persistence path uses this conversion too.
- **Per-turn INFO logs are now DEBUG.** Every turn logged four INFO lines, even for an agent with no tools: "Discovering httpcall tools…", "Discovered 0 httpcall tools…" and the two `mcpcalls` equivalents. Lines that fire on every turn when their feature is configured moved to DEBUG as well: built-in tool counts and lazy-tool loading (`AgentOrchestrator`, `ToolLoopRunner`), the contextual memory/recall/attachment tools, RAG and rolling-summary injection (`LlmTask`, `RagContextProvider`), the httpcall tool result line, and the cascade step lines. The string-concatenating ones now use `debugf`, so nothing is built while DEBUG is off.
- **No more `new ObjectMapper()` per discover_tools result (WS8.5).** `ToolLoopRunner.activateDiscoveredTools` built a mapper for every result; it now uses a shared one.
- **Bound Jackson readers and writers.** The Mongo `JacksonCodec` binds an `ObjectReader` for its type and an `ObjectWriter` once, instead of resolving them through the mapper for every document. `JsonSerialization` does the same for its writer and untyped reader.

### Measurements

Conversion, single thread, best of five (memory with ten properties and twelve data per step):

| Steps | Full conversion (persist) before → after | Turn response before → after |
|---|---|---|
| 10 | 8.0 → 6.6 µs | ≥ 8 → 1.0 µs |
| 50 | 57.8 → 30.4 µs | ≥ 58 → 2.6 µs |
| 200 | 376 → 113 µs | ≥ 376 → 2.5 µs |

"Turn response before" is at least the old full conversion, which the projection ran first. End to end (`scripts/loadtest`, `origin/main` against this branch, alternating A/B/A/B on Docker Desktop on a shared machine, so treat the numbers as indicative): steady 50 conversations with a 0 ms mock LLM, 81.9 → 102.9 turns/s on average (the rounds overlap: 88.7 and 75.1 against 120.1 and 85.7); long-conversation (5 × 200 turns), p50 87 → 80 ms at the same throughput, since MongoDB dominates there. INFO lines per run: 16,043 → 43.

### Tests

`ConversationMemoryCurrentStepConversionTest`: 168 golden-equivalence cases compare the new path with the full conversion plus trim across six memories (plain, secret last turn, secret earlier turn, error, redo, single step) × `returnDetailed` × `returnCurrentStepOnly` × seven `returningFields` shapes. One test checks that the path still redacts credentials, drops `audit:*` and masks a secret turn. One guard checks that only the newest step is read. Mutation-checked: reading the steps in the wrong order fails 51 cases, converting eagerly fails the guard, and dropping the redo cache fails 14 cases.

### Not done here

PR #1066 adds a `conversationCost` sum over **all** steps to `convertSimpleConversationMemory`. It stays correct on this path, because the lazy list converts every step it is asked for, but it gives up most of the saving for the default response. Once both land, the cost sum should read `audit:cost` from the live steps (or from a running total) rather than from converted snapshots.

**Files:** [`ConversationMemoryUtilities.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationMemoryUtilities.java), [`JacksonCodec.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/datastore/mongo/codec/JacksonCodec.java), [`JsonSerialization.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/datastore/serialization/JsonSerialization.java), [`ToolLoopRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java), [`HttpCallToolsProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/HttpCallToolsProvider.java), [`McpToolsProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/McpToolsProvider.java), `AgentOrchestrator`, `ContextualToolsProvider`, `LlmTask`, `RagContextProvider`, `CascadingModelExecutor`.

## test(loadtest): cluster mode for the turn load test, N replicas behind nginx (2026-10-17)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `test/loadtest-cluster-scaling`

### What changed and why

Cluster mode (NATS leases with fencing, the cluster event bus, degraded mode) is well tested for
correctness, but nothing measured multi-node throughput: the single-node WS2 report was the only data,
and the sizing claims in [clustering.md](../clustering.md#sizing), including "the database is the
shared bottleneck", were unverified.

- **`scripts/loadtest/docker-compose.cluster.yml`** (new): the topology of the repository's
  `docker-compose.cluster.yml` and `scripts/cluster-demo` (N EDDI replicas with
  `eddi.messaging.type=nats`, three NATS nodes at R3, one MongoDB, nginx round-robin), plus the
  WireMock LLM and k6. It has its own ports (75xx), so it runs next to the single-node stack.
- **`scripts/loadtest/nginx-cluster.conf`** (new): the cluster balancer's rules, sized for thousands
  of connections. It adds `X-Loadtest-Upstream` to every response, and only a refused connection moves
  on to the next replica, so 409s and 503s reach k6 and get counted.
- **`k6/turns.js`**: with `NODE_ADDRS` set, every turn metric is tagged with the replica that answered,
  and one no-op threshold per node makes k6 write the per-node submetrics into its summary.
- **`loadtest.py`**: new commands `cluster-up --nodes N`, `cluster-run`, `cluster-down`,
  `cluster-matrix --nodes 1,2,4,8 [--baseline]` and `cluster-report`. Every point starts from a fresh
  stack and gets a warm-up run that is thrown away. Each run collects, per node: turns/s, p50/p95/p99,
  409/503, `eddi_cluster_lease_acquire_seconds` by outcome, `eddi_cluster_fence_rejected_total`, peak
  in-flight turns and leases held. Across the cluster it collects MongoDB ops per turn and per second,
  NATS messages per turn, and the CPU of every container (other stacks' too) plus the host's.
  `--baseline` adds one single node with `eddi.messaging.type=in-memory`, so the overhead of cluster
  mode at N=1 shows up.

### Design decisions

- **Comparability is explicit.** In-flight turns per node (`--max-in-flight`, 400), the MongoDB
  `maxPoolSize` (100), heap, memory and CPUs per node are passed on every point. None of them is left
  to `max-in-flight-turns=auto`, which depends on the heap.
- **Host CPU is measured, not assumed.** On Docker Desktop, the replicas, MongoDB, NATS, nginx and k6
  share one VM with everything else on the machine. The report gives the host's CPU (typeperf on
  Windows) and other stacks' container CPU next to the efficiency column, because on one machine a
  falling efficiency can come from the host as easily as from the database.
- **Agent setup goes to a node directly.** Right after start, the balancer can still hold an address
  it resolved before the replica was up, and it answers the setup POST with a 502.
- **MongoDB gets `restart: on-failure`.** On a busy host the image's two-step first start can fail
  with exit 48 (address in use).

### Results

[`scripts/loadtest/results/cluster-scaling-2026-10-18.md`](../../scripts/loadtest/results/cluster-scaling-2026-10-18.md).
The setup was 100 conversations per node, a 3 s mock LLM and 2 CPUs per node. The host was saturated
(85–99 % CPU, shared with other sessions' work), so the report measures overhead and shape, not true
horizontal scaling.

- **Errors:** none across 18k turns — no 409, no 503, no fence rejection.
- **Overhead at N=1:** cluster mode ran 16.1 turns/s against 21.3 in memory (−24 %, +1.4 s p50). Every
  turn paid an uncontended lease acquire of ~151 ms.
- **Lease contention:** round-robin without affinity makes 13–32 % of acquires contended (0.5–2.6 s
  extra) even with one sequential client per conversation. A turn holds its lease until persisted,
  which is after the reply.
- **Efficiency:** 1.11 at N=2 (a quiet re-run; the first N=2 run read 0.28), 0.65 at N=4, 0.40 at N=8.
- **Where it saturated:** node CPU at N=4, then host CPU at N=8. MongoDB peaked at 1.2k ops/s
  (23 ops/turn, flat in N, 2.25 of 4 CPUs) and NATS at 0.5 CPU per node, so neither was the limit.
  The expected database knee is untested here.
- **NATS cost:** about 14–22 ms of NATS CPU per turn across R3. That puts a 1-vCPU NATS node nearer
  150–200 turns/s than the docs' "thousands", and needs a re-check on a quiet machine.

### Follow-ups

Profile the uncontended lease acquire. Decide between releasing the lease before post-reply
persistence and routing by conversation in the reference nginx config. Re-measure NATS CPU and the
database knee on separate machines.

---

## perf(engine): fewer MongoDB round trips per conversation turn (2026-10-16)

**Repo:** EDDI (`perf/turn-mongo-roundtrips`)

A JProfiler profile of the virtual-thread build (labsai/EDDI#1045) counted about 17 MongoDB reads per turn, each with a named caller, and found the driver's connection pool to be the ceiling once turns run on virtual threads (418 operations queued for a connection at 500 in-flight turns). This branch removes the redundant ones, one commit each, in the order the profile suggested.

### What changed and why

- **One conversation-descriptor read per request.** A REST turn read the same descriptor three times before it started: `RestAgentEngine`'s ownership check, the same check again in `ConversationService.say`, and the soft-delete check right after it. New `ConversationDescriptorMemo` (`@RequestScoped`) remembers the resolved descriptor (live, archived or none) for the rest of the request; `ConversationAccessGuard` and `ConversationService.isSoftDeleted` both resolve through it. Only the descriptor is shared, never a decision: each check still compares the owner against its own identity. Store failures are not remembered, every write through `ConversationDescriptorStore` forgets the conversation, and outside a request (worker threads, schedules) every lookup reads the store as before. Saves 2 reads per turn on the request thread, where the profile saw most of the pool waits.
- **`updateTimeStamp` is one update (WS3c).** It was `getCurrentResourceId` + `readDescriptor` + `ModifiableHistorizedResourceStore.set` (which reads again) + a version-checked full replace: 3 reads and a write on every turn's completion path. New storage SPI method `IResourceStorage.setNumberField(id, field, long)`: MongoDB `updateOne` with `$set`, PostgreSQL `UPDATE … SET data = jsonb_set(…, to_jsonb(?::bigint))`, both on the live row only (a soft-deleted or unknown id writes nothing). `DescriptorStore.touchLastModified` uses it; `ConversationDescriptorStore.updateTimeStamp` falls back to the old rewrite only for a backend without it. The value is stored as a number, exactly as the persistence mapper writes dates (epoch millis), so retention (`RestConversationStore` compares `getLastModifiedOn()`) and the listings' server-side sort see no difference — `DescriptorTouchParityTest` checks the read-back date, the stored type (BSON int64 / jsonb number), the sort position among fully written descriptors and that no other field or version changes, on a real MongoDB and PostgreSQL. A side benefit: the replace could overwrite a concurrent edit of any other descriptor field with the copy it had read; a single-field update cannot. A failure is now logged and swallowed instead of thrown into the completion callback, where it would have broken the response of a turn that was already stored.
- **Signing negative cache (WS8.1).** `AgentSigningService.loadPrivateKey` cached only keys it found, so with an active vault every audit entry of a keyless agent (three per turn) asked the vault — a `secretvault_secrets` read — only to hear "not found" again. A "not found" is now remembered for 60 s per key (`MISSING_KEY_TTL_NANOS`, bounded at 10 000 entries). A vault *failure* is never remembered. Generating or rotating a key on this node evicts the entry, so the new key signs at once — including when a lookup that started before the key was stored answers "not found" after the eviction: every eviction is counted, and a miss whose lookup saw the count move is withdrawn instead of remembered; a key created on another node is picked up within the TTL, and entries written meanwhile carry no signature, as they would have a moment earlier. This is also step 7 of PQ-5a in the PQC plan — implemented once, here.
- **Pinned agent configuration from memory.** `ConversationHitlService.lookupAgentConfigPinned` read the agent document at the conversation's pinned version on every turn, only to learn its tool-approval policy. New `AgentConfigurationCache` (`@ApplicationScoped`, Caffeine, 1 000 entries, 30 min after last access) keeps `(agentId, version)` configurations: a stored version never changes (edits write a new version; the only in-place writers are the descriptor stores and the startup migrations). Absence and store failures are not cached, so the HITL gate still fails closed on a read error and still falls back to the latest version when the pinned one is gone. `AgentFactory` drops an agent's entries on every deploy and undeploy. `WorkflowTraversal`'s own short-lived traversal cache is left as it is; WS1 should hang its invalidation off the same deployment hooks.
- **One full conversation load per turn (WS3a).** The conversation-id entry points (`say` / `sayStreaming` without an environment — REST, SSE, MCP, Slack, `/v1`) load the snapshot to find the agent and run the pre-turn checks, then called the environment-qualified overload, whose admission (`admitTurn`) loaded the same document again. That load is now handed down (`preloaded`) and converted instead of re-read. The idempotency admission (`admitOrServe`) still runs between the load and the turn, as before; a deferred duplicate re-enters the whole entry point and loads afresh. The environment-qualified overloads (A2A, group members, tests) still load on their own. Staleness is unchanged: the second load used to happen a moment after the first, and the step runner's `reloadIfSuperseded` revision check still guards the turn either way.

### Measured

Load-test harness from labsai/EDDI#1044 (`steady`, 50 conversations × 20 turns, mock LLM delay 0 ms, everything in Docker), `origin/main` (`aa8806c68`) against this branch, 1 000 turns each. MongoDB operations per successful turn (`serverStatus().opcounters`, per-collection from `top`):

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| **Queries per turn** | **16.67** | **6.65** |
| `descriptors` queries | 6.05 | 1.05 |
| `conversationmemories` queries | 5.04 | 4.08 |
| `secretvault_secrets` queries | 3.05 | 0.06 |
| `agents` queries | 1.09 | 0.09 |
| Updates / inserts per turn | 2.05 / 3.11 | 2.05 / 3.11 |
| Turns/s (single run, indicative) | 117 | 146 |

On PostgreSQL (`pg_stat_statements`, `SELECT conversation_state, data FROM conversation_memories`), full conversation loads per turn went from 2.09 to 1.13 with short conversations, and from 2.74 to 1.88 in the `long-conversation` scenario (10 × 200 turns). An instrumented build traced the excess over MongoDB's 2.0 to `ConversationStepRunner.reloadIfSuperseded`: the reply is delivered before the turn is persisted, and on PostgreSQL the full-document append grows with the conversation, so the client's next turn is often admitted against the row as it was before the previous turn's write and reloaded when it runs (68 % of turns on `main`). That reload is what keeps the turn on the current document, so it stays; avoiding it means loading at run time when a turn is already in flight, or a cheaper PostgreSQL write (WS6/WS7).

The descriptor write stays one per turn, now a `$set` instead of a replace. Left on the turn path: one descriptor read (the ownership check), the full conversation load, the state and revision projections, and the GDPR restriction check on `usermemories`.

### Design decisions

- **A request-scoped memo, not new parameters.** The checks sit behind `IConversationService.say` / `sayStreaming`, which Slack, MCP, `/v1` and A2A also call; threading a resolved descriptor through all of them would have changed every signature for a saving that only the REST path needs twice.

## 🔒 fix(ui): clear three npm advisories reported by Scorecard (2026-10-16)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/ui-dependency-advisories`)

### What changed and why

OpenSSF Scorecard's Vulnerabilities check flagged three advisories. All three
came from the UI lockfiles, not from the Maven build.

- **GHSA-238p-pmpm-9mq7, KaTeX prototype-pollution trust bypass (`katex` < 0.18.2).**
  The Chat UI's own `katex` was already 0.19.0. `rehype-katex@7.0.1` and
  `micromark-extension-math@3.1.0` each ask for `^0.16`, which gave them a nested
  0.16.47 copy that ships in the chat bundle. Both are on their latest release,
  so an npm `overrides` entry now points both at the root version (`$katex`).
  Build, lint and all 480 chat tests pass, including the `MessageBubble`
  math-rendering tests.
- **GHSA-rj75-hqrm-r3gf, quadratic selector parsing in `postcss-selector-parser`
  (< 7.1.6).** `@tailwindcss/typography@0.5.20`, also on its latest release,
  pins exactly 6.0.10. The Manager now overrides it to `^7.1.6`. This is a
  build-time dev dependency. The Manager's generated CSS is byte-for-byte
  identical under 6.0.10 and 7.1.6, across all three stylesheets and 141
  `.prose` rules.
- **GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm, `braces` stack exhaustion (all versions through 3.0.3,
  with no fixed release).** It reached the repo only through
  `ui/manager/.ds-sync/package-lock.json`, via `@tailwindcss/cli` →
  `@parcel/watcher` → `micromatch`. `.ds-sync` holds local design-sync tooling
  that is never shipped and is not inventoried in the SBOM. Its lockfile is
  removed and gitignored, and the Dependabot entry for that directory stays to
  keep the manifest's direct dependencies current.

Both lockfiles were regenerated with the pinned Node 24.21.0 (npm 11.19.0), so
the diff holds only the dependency change, without `peer`-flag churn from a
different npm. Each nested entry was deleted by hand before `npm install`,
because npm does not apply a new override to an entry the lockfile has already
resolved.

**Files:** [`ui/chat/package.json`](../../ui/chat/package.json),
[`ui/manager/package.json`](../../ui/manager/package.json), both
`package-lock.json` files, [`ui/manager/.gitignore`](../../ui/manager/.gitignore),
[`.github/dependabot.yml`](../../.github/dependabot.yml)

### Next

Drop the two overrides once `rehype-katex`/`micromark-extension-math` accept
KaTeX ≥ 0.18.2 and `@tailwindcss/typography` stops pinning
`postcss-selector-parser` 6.
