## docs(planning): human takeover / handoff design for review (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`docs/planning-human-takeover`; planning document only, no production code)

### What changed and why

WS7 of the competitor-research roadmap needs a maintainer-approved design before any code: a live 1:1 conversation handed to a person, who talks to the user while the pipeline is off and then hands it back. [`planning/human-takeover-design.md`](../../planning/human-takeover-design.md) is that design, written against `origin/main` @ `aa811c838`.

- **Inventory of every branch on `ConversationState`.** Engine, both conversation stores, cluster leases and fencing (#956), idempotent turns (#1000), the idle sweep and undeploy (#990), HITL, `/v1`, Slack, A2A, MCP, group members, schedules, Chat UI and Manager. Each entry gives the location, what it does today and what the new state needs. Allow-list sites are safe by default. The deny-list sites are listed as must-fix: `NON_APPENDABLE_STATES` in both stores, undo/redo, the admin state reset, the per-agent idle sweep, `TurnIdempotencyService.isReplayable`, and the A2A outcome `switch`, whose `default -> completed` would report an empty finished task.
- **Recommendations:** a new `HUMAN_CONTROL` state rather than reusing `AWAITING_HUMAN`; the reserved action `HANDOFF_TO_HUMAN`, applied at the end of the turn; an opt-in `request_human_agent` tool that is never selected by an empty built-in whitelist; operator endpoints under `/agents/{conversationId}/takeover`. Takeover writes run as coordinator tasks under the conversation lease, which is never held for the length of a takeover. One step per message, with the operator's identity kept out of user-visible output. `operator.*` audit entries with the actor set server-side and message text stored only as a digest (a content commitment, not a copy). Because `AuditLedgerService` writes asynchronously, each takeover write also records its audit entry in a journal inside the conversation document, and a reconciler submits any entry a crash lost. Operator turns go into LLM history annotated as a human's. A dedicated role that never admits the conversation owner and, by default, reaches only the agents whose desk the caller belongs to.
- **Separable first PR:** a property patch alongside a HITL `/resume` decision. It is allow-listed per agent, refuses `longTerm` and `secret` scopes, is audited by digest, and is validated before the resume CAS so a refused patch does not use up the pause.

### Design decisions

- `AWAITING_HUMAN` means "refuse input, then resume the pipeline at a bookmark", and HITL admits the owner. Takeover means "accept input, return to idle", and the owner must be excluded. Reusing the state would take a sub-branch at about 20 sites, and a missed one silently drops customer messages.
- Paths avoid the word "operator", because in the Manager the Operator is the Platform Operator LLM.

### What's next

Maintainer sign-off on the eleven questions in §11 of the design (Q11 confirms the secure default: desk scoping required, unrestricted access only by explicit opt-in), then PR 1 (property patch) and PR 2 (state and API) as laid out in §13.

---

## feat(agent-tests): agent regression harness — golden conversations (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/agent-test-harness`; WS6 Phase 6.1)

### What changed and why

EDDI had thousands of tests and none that asserted what an *agent* does. A config-driven engine records every turn — actions, properties, output, tool calls, cost — so a suite can assert exactly that, independently of how an LLM words its answer.

- **`AgentTestSuite` config resource** (`configs/agenttests`): a versioned document like every other config — scripted cases of turns, per-turn expectations, tool mocks, defaults (`toolMode`, `unmockedTools`, `repetitions`, `passRate`, `turnTimeoutSeconds`) and a per-run dollar ceiling (`maxCostPerRun`, default $1). `AgentTestSuiteValidator` refuses at save time (400) what a run could not honour: unknown modes, invalid or over-long regexes (500 chars), more than 200 cases / 50 turns per case / 20 repetitions / 2,000 turns per run.
- **`AgentTestRun` records** (`engine/agenttests`, collection `agenttestruns`): one document per run through `IResourceStorageFactory` at a single version (the `GroupConversationStore` pattern), so it works on MongoDB and PostgreSQL alike; listed per agent newest-first, pruned to a bounded number per agent.
- **`AgentTestRunner`** runs a suite against an agent version deployed to the `test` environment: one conversation per case repetition as user `agent-test:<runId>:<case>:<repetition>`, each turn through the non-streaming `say` with a per-turn timeout, judged from the stored memory afterwards (`actions`, properties, output text, `audit:tool_calls`, `audit:cost`, state). Repetitions run on virtual threads, at most `eddi.agent-tests.max-parallel-cases` (4) at once; the summed `audit:cost` (start turns included) is checked against `maxCostPerRun` before every turn. A HITL pause is an observable `conversationState`, after which the conversation is ended — the runner never waits for a human. Every test conversation is ended and permanently deleted afterwards (`RestConversationStore.purgeConversation`, the guard-free half of the permanent delete) unless the run keeps them. One summary audit-ledger entry per run (`taskId: ai.labs.agenttest`).
- **`ExpectationEvaluator`**: `actionsContain`, `actionsExact`, `properties` (`equals` — representation-tolerant, `3` = `"3"` — `matches`, `exists`, `absent`), `outputContains`, `outputMatches`, `toolCalled` (+ `argsSubset`), `toolNotCalled`, `maxToolCalls`, `maxCostUsd`, `conversationState`. Every failing expectation is reported with the expected and actual value.
- **ReDoS guard (`BoundedRegex`)**: no safe-regex helper existed. Patterns are capped at 500 characters at save time and matched against a `CharSequence` that throws after 250 ms, so a catastrophically backtracking pattern fails its expectation instead of occupying a thread.
- **Long-term user memory is neither read nor written by test conversations**: `ConversationService.createPropertiesHandler` hands an `agent-test:` user no user-memory store (so `Conversation` skips both the init load and `storePropertiesPermanently`), and the undo/redo long-term sync skips them.
- **`AgentTestService`** (shared by REST and MCP): reading the suite needs VIEW; the version under test is deployed to `test` through `IRestAgentAdministration.deployAgent(test, …, autoDeploy=false, wait)`, so the deploy's own EDIT check, space-reference guard and agent quota apply to the starter; a run is refused (409) when a newer version is also deployed to `test`, because new conversations start on the newest ready version. At most `eddi.agent-tests.max-concurrent-runs` (2) runs execute per node (429 beyond).
- **Tool mocking (`ToolMockRegistry`), fail closed.** Consulted at the three places a pipeline reaches the outside world: `ToolLoopRunner.executeSingleToolCallResult` before executor dispatch (every LLM tool source; the HITL resume path goes through the same method), `ApiCallExecutor.execute` (rule-triggered httpcalls — the mock replaces the `IResponse`, so `saveResponse`, retries and `postResponse` property instructions run on the mocked `{httpCode, body}` unchanged; fire-and-forget calls are not dispatched) and `McpCallsTask` (rule-triggered MCP calls — no server contact at all, not even tool discovery, unless the suite runs live). An unmocked call returns `{"status":"NOT_EXECUTED","reason":"no mock defined in test mode"}` (a 501 for httpcalls) and fails the case; `toolMode: live` / `unmockedTools: live` are explicit opt-ins. A test conversation the registry does not know — a turn still running after its run gave up — is refused too. Memory-writing tools never run live in a test. For every other conversation the hook is one string prefix check on the user id. The LLM-path hook is a self-contained early return directly after the call is traced, so the pending split of that method merges trivially.
- **Tool expectations see all three paths**: the registry records every intercepted call per turn (mocked, refused or live), and `toolCalled` / `toolNotCalled` / `maxToolCalls` are judged against that record rather than the LLM-only `audit:tool_calls` trace.
- **REST.** Suites: `/testsuitestore/testsuites` (URI `eddi://ai.labs.testsuite/testsuitestore/testsuites/{id}?version=N`, the v6 store convention; registered with the workspace access-index backfill). Runs: `POST /agenttests/runs` (202 with the `RUNNING` record), `GET /agenttests/runs/{runId}`, `GET /agenttests/runs?agentId=`. Both `eddi-admin`/`eddi-editor`, the deploy tier.
- **MCP.** `run_agent_tests`, `get_agent_test_run`, `list_agent_test_runs` (`McpAgentTestTools`, AUTHOR tier like `deploy_agent`); tool count 85 → 88.
- **Metrics.** `eddi_agent_tests_runs_total{status}`, `eddi_agent_tests_turns_total`, `eddi_agent_tests_run_duration_seconds`, charted in the Scheduling row of the Full Metrics Reference dashboard.
- **Docs.** New [`agent-testing.md`](../agent-testing.md) (concepts, JSON reference, mocking rules, CI `curl` recipe, EU AI Act evidence note); `mcp-server.md`, `metrics.md`, `configuration-reference.md` (`eddi.agent-tests.*`), `SUMMARY.md`.
- **Full-engine proof:** `AgentTestSuiteIT` (DevServices MongoDB) stores a suite over REST, starts a run against the deterministic rules-only `agentengine` fixture agent — the run deploys it to `test` itself — and reads back `PASSED` for a suite that holds and `FAILED` with the actual actions for one that does not. It found two defects the unit tests could not: MongoDB assigns a run's id only on its first write, and a turn's completion callback can run before the turn is stored, so a turn is now judged from the snapshot `say` completes with (detailed, current step) and only its internal `audit:cost`/model names — stripped from every client projection — are read from the stored step, waiting up to 5 s for it.
- **Platform Operator.** May read suites and runs and start a run (a gated write — it deploys to `test`, like the deploy it already has); it may not write suites. Prompt explains reading results and `unmockedTool`. `operator-revision.json` → 24.

### Review fixes

- **Unchecked purge moved out of the REST layer.** The permanent delete is now `ConversationPurgeService` (memory package); `RestConversationStore` calls `purge` only after its owner check and keeps no unchecked public method. The runner calls `purgeAgentTestConversation(conversationId, runId)`, which refuses any conversation whose user id is not `agent-test:<runId>:…` — the runner can only delete what it created. Nothing in the delete path reads a request identity (tested on an identity-less virtual thread, with a pending approval ended as a system actor).
- **Cluster mode is refused (409).** Mocks and runs live in one JVM and NATS cluster mode can route a test turn to another node. A call refused for want of a session on this node now says so (`no agent-test session on this node …`) instead of `no mock defined`.
- **PR review.** A run whose result cannot be stored (an oversized document, say) is replaced by a terminal `ERROR` record without the per-case detail, never left `RUNNING`; an output expectation's failure records the output cut at 2,000 characters like the turn's own; a suite read goes through the shared `RestVersionInfo.read` (`version=0` = current, null/negative checks); the purge treats the store's `null` for a missing conversation as already gone. A rule-triggered httpcall's mock is selected before the request is built, so a mocked call neither resolves `${vault:}`/`${connection:}`/`${caller:}` references nor fails for an unprovisioned credential.
- **Orphaned runs.** A new `ABORTED` status: at startup, `AgentTestRunRecovery` marks every `RUNNING` run (none can be live yet) aborted with the reason and deletes its test conversations, found by their deterministic user ids.

### Design decisions

- **The test marker is the user id** (`agent-test:` prefix), not a new memory field: the `CONVERSATION_START` turn runs inside `startConversation` before the conversation has an id, and the user id is the one thing every seam (user-memory handler, tool loop, httpcall executor, MCP task) already has. The prefix only ever takes capabilities away, so it need not be unforgeable. `agent-test:` rather than the plan's `test:`, which a real deployment could plausibly use for real users.
- **`ToolMockRegistry` is static**, because the LLM tool loop is plain classes built by `AgentOrchestrator`; threading a CDI bean through would have changed a dozen constructors (and the pending `executeToolCall` split).
- **Runs are not versioned config** and live in their own single-version collection, pruned per agent (`eddi.agent-tests.max-runs-per-agent`, 50).
- **A newer version deployed to `test` refuses the run** instead of being undeployed: the runner never changes a deployment it did not make.
- **Suites are not yet part of agent export ZIPs** (maintainer decision) — follow-up.

### Known gaps and follow-ups

- Phase 6.2: generate a suite from a conversation, LLM-as-judge expectation, Manager page. Phase 6.3: optional deploy gate.
- Suites in agent export/import ZIPs and in agent sync.
- A run lives on the node that started it; if that node stops, the run is marked `ABORTED` at the next startup and must be started again.

**Files:** [`AgentTestSuite.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/agenttests/model/AgentTestSuite.java), [`AgentTestSuiteValidator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/agenttests/AgentTestSuiteValidator.java), [`AgentTestSuiteStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/agenttests/mongo/AgentTestSuiteStore.java), [`AgentTestRunStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestRunStore.java), [`AgentTestRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestRunner.java), [`AgentTestService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/AgentTestService.java), [`ExpectationEvaluator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/ExpectationEvaluator.java), [`ConversationService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationService.java), [`ToolMockRegistry.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/ToolMockRegistry.java), [`IRestAgentTestRuns.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/agenttests/IRestAgentTestRuns.java), [`IRestAgentTestSuiteStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/agenttests/IRestAgentTestSuiteStore.java), [`McpAgentTestTools.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpAgentTestTools.java), [`agent-testing.md`](../agent-testing.md)

## feat(groups): `member_tool_call` SSE event for group members (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/groups-member-tool-call-events`)

### What changed and why

A group member's turn is not streamed, so an observer saw nothing between
`speaker_start` and `speaker_complete`, however long the member's tool loop ran.
The 1:1 stream already emits `tool_call` (name only); the group stream now emits
`member_tool_call` `{agentId, displayName, tool}` with the same rule: the name
travels, the arguments never do.

- `IConversationMemory` gains a name-only `toolCallObserver` (default null),
  deliberately **separate from the event sink**: a non-null sink switches the LLM
  task into its streaming path, which a member turn must not trigger.
  `ToolLoopRunner` notifies it next to the sink (observer failures are swallowed).
- `ConversationResponseHandler.toolCallObserver()` (default null) lets a plain
  `say` caller supply it; `ConversationService` installs it per turn.
  `MemberTurnExecutor` supplies one only when a listener exists, and drops events
  from a cancelled speaker (the callback runs on a coordinator thread).
- `GroupDiscussionEventListener.onMemberToolCall` (default no-op), emitted by
  `RestGroupConversation`. `SlackGroupDiscussionListener` keeps the no-op: Slack
  posts finished contributions, and a message per tool call would be noise.
- Manager: the speaking member's typing indicator reads "using X..." instead of
  "responding..." (i18n in all locales). Docs: SSE table gains `member_tool_call`,
  `cost_updated`, `stance_updated`, and `token` is marked as never emitted.

### Design decisions

- Observer on memory rather than a new `say` overload: keeps the 9-argument
  `say` signature (and every existing mock of it) intact.
- Absent observer or listener changes nothing.

---

## ✨ feat(llm): input and output guardrails on LLM tasks (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/llm-input-output-guardrails`) — WS4 Phase 4a, executing the R0 spec of `planning/langchain4j-recommendations.md`.

### What changed and why

Only tool *results* were governed (`ToolResultGuardrail`). Nothing screened the user's message or the model's final answer — the largest governance gap (jailbreaks, PII leaking either way). An LLM task can now carry `inputGuardrails` and `outputGuardrails`: ordered lists of `{type, action, maxRetries, blockMessage, parameters}`. Actions are `block` (default), `redact`, `reprompt` (output: re-ask the same model, `maxRetries` default 2, clamped 0–5, then block) and `warn`. Built-in types: `prompt-injection` (input), `pii-redaction`, `regex-filter`, `max-length` (both), `json-object` (output). Nothing is on by default; a task without the lists never touches the guardrail service.

- **Input** guardrails screen the user's raw message **before retrieval** (a blocked message issues no RAG query or `httpCallRag` call; a redacted one is the retrieval query), then the assembled prompt before the model is built, `preRequest` runs or the circuit breaker is consulted — the second pass reuses the first result when the model-facing message is unchanged, so a finding is recorded once. A block answers the turn with the block message, served as a fallback (`LlmFallbackHandler.serveText`: the step's `llm:fallback:<taskId>` flag keeps it out of later history, `fallbackField` wrapping applies under `convertToObject`); the model is never called. Since the history builder merges a fallback turn's question into the next user message, only the last text part of the last user message is the current turn — earlier parts are screened as history, so a blocked injection cannot block every later turn. Redaction rewrites what the model sees; memory keeps the user's text. Earlier user messages of the history window are re-screened silently every turn, so a message blocked/redacted on its own turn is not replayed to the model later (a guardrail can opt out via `screensHistory()`, meant for model-backed types).
- **Output** guardrails run right after `responseValidation`, in every branch — plain, streaming, tool loop, cascade — and on the HITL resume path. A block is served through the same `serveText` path (flagged, wrapped, not parsed, not recorded as the model's response); the raw output stored under `langchain:<type>:<taskId>` is the wrapped content, as on every other fallback path, while the stream and the visible output carry only the message text. A reprompt runs on the branch that produced the answer: plain chat re-asks the model; the tool loop and a resumed loop re-ask only the final answer over the tool transcript (`reaskFinalAnswer` — no tool runs twice); the cascade re-asks the accepted step. Corrective shape is R5's (`FormatRetryRunner.withCorrective`): request, rejected answer as assistant turn, corrective user message. A reprompt needs `responseValidation.minAttemptMs` of turn time left (#996 deadline) and an admitting per-model circuit breaker (#1001: a fresh ticket per reprompt, settled with its own outcome; the HITL resume neither consults nor settles one, as for R12), else blocks. Reprompt tokens fold into the turn's token usage and cost (cascade: priced with the accepted step's prices into `cascadeCostUsd`).
- **Streaming:** an output guardrail with `block`/`redact`/`reprompt` forces the task onto the buffered path exactly like a `retry` response-validation policy (`bufferOutput = retryPolicy != null || guardrailBuffering` replaces every streaming decision that used `retryPolicy != null`); `warn` keeps streaming.
- **Evidence:** step key `audit:guardrail:<phase>:<type>` (a list of `{phase,type,action,reason,retryCount,taskId}`), collected by `LifecycleManager.buildAuditEntry` into `AuditEntry.output.guardrails` (LLM task only); SSE `guardrail_triggered` `{type,phase,action,taskId}` via `ConversationEventSink` → `StreamingResponseHandler` (both wrappers in `ConversationService`) → `RestAgentEngineStreaming`; counter `eddi.guardrail.triggered{phase,type,action}`.

### Design decisions

- **EDDI-native SPI instead of `dev.langchain4j.guardrail` types.** R0 chose the lc4j types; against langchain4j 1.22.0 (`javap`-verified) `OutputGuardrailRequest` still demands a `ChatExecutor` that a guardrail may call itself — a second model-call path outside the turn's budget, deadline and cost accounting — `GuardrailRequestParams` still NPEs without `userMessageTemplate`/`variables`, results are sealed, and `JsonExtractorOutputGuardrail` no longer exists. Reprompting is EDDI's loop through a `Reprompter` the branch supplies, so the executor would be dead weight or a hazard. `ContentGuardrail` (`type()`, `phases()`, `evaluate(text, context)` → `GuardrailVerdict` ALLOW/REWRITE/VIOLATION) keeps "a new type is just another bean": `GuardrailService` indexes every `@Any Instance<ContentGuardrail>` by type. `GuardrailVerdict.costUsd` and `GuardrailContext.messages` are the hooks Phase 4b's `classifier` type needs.
- **A block is a value, never an exception** (R0 T1): a guardrail message can quote user text and would otherwise reach the tool loop's retry classifier.
- **Fail closed** (Pillar 4, consistent with the 4b classifier): a guardrail that throws or returns no verdict blocks (reason `guardrail_error`) when its action is `block` or `reprompt`, and warns when it is `redact` or `warn`; per-guardrail `parameters.failMode` (`closed`/`open`) overrides. History messages a closed guardrail cannot check are withheld.
- **Find vs act:** guardrails only report; for `pii-redaction`/`regex-filter`/`max-length` a finding with `block` refuses, with `redact` applies the rewrite.
- **No new loop on top of `FormatRetryRunner`:** its triggers are a fixed enum tied to `responseValidation`; reusing its corrective shape, `Asker`, `turnBudget` and `minAttemptMs` keeps the semantics without entangling the two policies.
- **No `ExtensionDescriptor` entries** (R0 1.3 f): the descriptor describes the workflow step's `uri`, not the LLM resource; guardrails live in the resource.
- **Not extracted into `executeModel`** (T11): hooked at the existing seams instead, so the diff in `LlmTask` is additive plus the `retryPolicy != null` → `bufferOutput` substitutions.

### Known limits / follow-ups

- An `httpCallRag` template that reads `memory.current.input` directly (instead of `userInput`) still sees the stored, unredacted text (documented).
- Manager: only the `LlmTask` type mirror (`inputGuardrails`/`outputGuardrails`, `GuardrailConfig`); the guardrail list editor on the LLM task form came with #1077, merged into this branch. Platform Operator cheatsheet explains configuring guardrails; revision bumped.
- Phase 4b (`classifier` type) builds on this branch.
- SSE acceptance criterion 11 (`guardrail_triggered` before `done` over a real socket) is covered at unit level (handler → `sendJson`) only.

**Files:** [`GuardrailService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/GuardrailService.java), [`ContentGuardrail.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/ContentGuardrail.java), built-ins under [`guardrails/impl/`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/impl/), [`LlmTask.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/LlmTask.java), [`LlmConfiguration.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/model/LlmConfiguration.java), [`LifecycleManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/lifecycle/internal/LifecycleManager.java), SSE plumbing (`ConversationEventSink`, `IConversationService`, `ConversationService`, `RestAgentEngineStreaming`); docs [`langchain.md`](../langchain.md#input-and-output-guardrails), [`security.md`](../security.md), [`audit-ledger.md`](../audit-ledger.md), [`metrics.md`](../metrics.md), [`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md).

## feat(llm): tool repeat guard refuses identical failing tool calls (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/llm-tool-repeat-guard`)

### What changed and why

Inside the iteration cap a model could re-send the same failing call (a 403, a 404, a schema-rejected body) every round, burning cost and rate-limit budget and repeating side effects on non-idempotent endpoints. The tool loop now keeps a per-run `ToolRepeatGuard`: a call whose last identical call failed, with no other call succeeding since, is answered `NOT_EXECUTED` without running and traced as `tool_repeat_refused` (metric `eddi.tool.repeat.refused`, tags `source`, `reason`). After `maxRefusalsPerTurn` (default 3) refusals the loop stops asking the model and ends the turn with a fixed message.

**Behaviour change: the guard is ON by default.** An agent with no `toolRepeatGuard` now gets it with the defaults (`maxIdenticalFailures` 2, `maxIdenticalCalls` unlimited, `maxRefusalsPerTurn` 3). It only refuses calls that already failed identically with no progress in between. `"toolRepeatGuard": {"enabled": false}` restores the previous behaviour exactly. See [Repeated failing tool calls](../langchain.md#repeated-failing-tool-calls).

- `ToolOutcomeClassifier` classifies the RAW tool result (before the provenance envelope): HTTP `httpCode` 2xx success, 408/425/429/5xx retryable, other statuses terminal; `Error:` strings and `{"error":...}` retryable, except rejected arguments and unknown tools (terminal); loop-level refusals (budget, tenant quota, expired deadline, `NOT_EXECUTED`/`DENIED`) never count as failures. MCP `isError` results reach the loop as an internal-error string and are retryable.
- `ToolLoopRunner.executeToolCall` returns governed and raw results; `executeSingleToolCallResult` still returns the governed string (resume path unchanged).
- Guard state is per run of the loop: a HITL resume and each cascade step start fresh. Failures were already never cached.
- Platform Operator prompt updated (revision bumped).

**Files:** [`ToolRepeatGuard.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolRepeatGuard.java), [`ToolOutcomeClassifier.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolOutcomeClassifier.java), [`ToolRepeatGuardConfig.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/model/ToolRepeatGuardConfig.java), [`ToolLoopRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java)

**Manager:** the LLM task editor's tool-call limits block has a "Repeated failing tool calls" control (toggle, thresholds, exempt tools); the field is written only when the author changes something.

## ✨ feat(observability): W3C trace propagation on outbound calls and execute_tool spans (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/observability-trace-propagation`)

### What changed and why

A trace used to stop at EDDI's edge: it showed that a turn spent four seconds in
the LLM task, but not which MCP server or API made it slow. Two additions close
that gap (pillar 6, observability).

- **`execute_tool {tool}` span per tool call** — opened in
  `ToolExecutionService.executeToolWrapped`, the one seam every tool source
  passes through (builtin, http, mcp, a2a, dynamic, memory, recall, plus the
  `mcpcalls` step). GenAI semconv attributes (`gen_ai.operation.name`,
  `gen_ai.tool.name`, `gen_ai.tool.call.id`, `eddi.tool.source`, `error.type`),
  semconv revision pinned via `LlmTelemetryListener.SEMCONV_SCHEMA_VERSION`. The
  span is *current* while the tool runs — inline, and on the timeout worker
  through the existing `carryContext` — so outbound requests are its children.
  Cache hits (`eddi.tool.cache_hit=true`) and rate-limited calls
  (`error.type=rate_limited`) get a span too: cheap when OTel is off, and exactly
  what a slow-turn investigation needs to see.
- **W3C `traceparent`/`tracestate` on outbound calls**, via a new
  `TracePropagation` bean (`W3CTraceContextPropagator` only — never `baggage`):
  httpcalls (`HttpClientWrapper`, on a copy of the request so the memory record
  and the HITL approval fingerprint do not change per send), the `OutboundHttp`
  facade — built-in tools, A2A, the RAG crawler, OAuth, Slack, remote sync
  (first hop and same-origin hops; dropped on cross-origin hops like
  credentials — the Vert.x redirect handler drops them too) — and MCP
  (`params._meta` per SEP-414 through langchain4j's `metaSupplier`, plus the
  HTTP header).
- **Quarkus' Vert.x HTTP client tracer is off for the httpcalls client**
  (`TracingPolicy.IGNORE`). With the default policy it re-injected a fresh
  `traceparent` onto every redirect hop, including a cross-origin one the
  redirect handler had just stripped. The explicit header still carries the
  trace; the auto-instrumented client span for httpcalls is what is given up.
- **Config:** `eddi.tracing.propagate-outbound` (default `true`). Inert while
  `quarkus.otel.sdk.disabled=true` — a no-op span has an invalid context and
  produces no header, never an all-zero one.

### Design decisions

- **MCP threading.** Verified in the langchain4j-mcp 1.22.0-beta32 bytecode:
  `DefaultMcpClient` calls the meta supplier synchronously on the calling thread
  before the transport, but `StreamableHttpMcpTransport` rebuilds the request on
  an HTTP-client callback thread when it retries after session expiry. So the
  header supplier reads the trace context **from the message's `_meta`**, not
  from `Context.current()` — header and `_meta` cannot disagree, whichever thread
  builds the request.
- **Source and call id travel in the OTel `Context`** (`ToolCallTrace`), set by
  `ToolLoopRunner` around its one call into the service, rather than as two more
  parameters: they label the span and nothing else, `ToolInvocation`'s equality
  is a cost/cache identity, and a new overload would have broken the Mockito
  stubs of 17 test classes.
- **Merged onto the `OutboundHttp` facade (#1042/#1046).** `SafeHttpClient`
  was retired on `main` while this branch was open; propagation moved into the
  facade's `exchange`/`redirectHeaders`, so every caller of the facade gets it
  and A2A no longer needs its own injection.
- **Tracer:** lazily resolved `GlobalOpenTelemetry.getTracer("eddi.tools")`, same
  pattern as `LlmTelemetryListener`/`LifecycleManager`; package-private field so
  tests install an SDK tracer without mutating the JVM-wide global.

### Docs

Monitoring guide: `execute_tool` span, propagation rules, and a "Sending traces
to Langfuse / Arize Phoenix / Jaeger / Tempo" section with endpoints verified
against each project's docs (Phoenix Cloud left explicitly unverified).
Configuration reference row; tracing note in `metrics.md`; Platform Operator
prompt line on tracing a slow tool (revision 17 → 18).

**Files:** [`TracePropagation.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/tracing/TracePropagation.java),
[`ToolExecutionService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/ToolExecutionService.java),
[`ToolCallTrace.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/ToolCallTrace.java),
[`OutboundHttp.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/httpclient/outbound/OutboundHttp.java),
[`HttpClientWrapper.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/httpclient/impl/HttpClientWrapper.java),
[`HttpClientModule.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/httpclient/bootstrap/HttpClientModule.java),
[`McpToolProviderManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/McpToolProviderManager.java),
[`A2AToolProviderManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/A2AToolProviderManager.java),
[`monitoring-guide.md`](../monitoring/monitoring-guide.md).

### Not verified

The acceptance check — one connected trace through the monitoring compose
(pipeline task → gen_ai span → execute_tool → downstream span) — was not run
end-to-end in a live stack; the unit tests pin each link.

## 💰 feat(tenancy): enforce the tenant monthly cost budget — meter settled turns and Dream runs, gate model calls (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/tenancy-cost-metering`)

### What changed and why

`maxMonthlyCostUsd` was stored, validated, shown in the Manager quotas page and documented as
**not enforced**: `TenantQuotaService.recordCost` had no production caller, so the monthly counter
stayed at zero and the pre-call gate never tripped. It is now real.

- **Metering at the settle points.** New [`TurnCostMeter`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/tenancy/TurnCostMeter.java)
  charges the current step's `audit:cost` to the default tenant from the pipeline's completion
  callback — `ConversationService.admitTurn` (say, streamed say, rerun; group member turns and
  sub-agents go through it too), the inline `CONVERSATION_START` turn in `startConversation`, and
  `ConversationHitlService.resumeConversation` (now always passes a completion callback, also for an
  internally driven resume). Only the not-yet-metered part is charged: the step keeps
  `audit:metered_cost`, persisted with the turn, so a turn paused for approval and resumed is charged
  once in total. Skipped for a turn stopped by lease loss (nothing is stored; the new owner re-runs
  and meters it); a replayed idempotent turn never reaches the callback. A denied or unavailable
  `recordCost` never fails the turn — logged and counted on `eddi.tenant.cost.metering{outcome}`;
  a charge the store could not record is carried forward as `audit:pending_cost` on the newest step
  and charged by the conversation's next settle (review finding: an ordinary say turn starts a new
  step, and say turns are persisted as appends, so leaving it on the old step would have lost it).
- **The marker key is `audit:metered_cost`, not `audit:cost:metered`.** Step lookups are prefix
  matches, so a key starting with `audit:cost` would be read back as the step's cost by
  `getLatestData(AUDIT_COST)` (`LlmTask.accumulateCost`, `LifecycleManager`).
- **`audit:cost` no longer depends on the audit ledger.** `LlmTask` only wrote it with
  `eddi.audit.enabled=true`; token usage and cost are now recorded either way
  (`accumulateUsageAndCost`), since the tenant budget charges them.
- **Pre-model-call gate.** New [`LlmSpendGate`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/LlmSpendGate.java),
  asked once per LLM task before any model request (plain, tool loop, cascade, streaming). The
  existing `checkCostBudget` gate ran only before tool calls, so an agent without tools never
  stopped, and one with tools kept calling its model to be refused tool after tool. A refusal fails
  soft like the tool-call gate: the model is not called, the task's fallback answer
  (`responseValidation.fallbackMessage`, or the default sentence) is served regardless of `onError`,
  the step records `llm:spend_refused:<taskId>`, and `eddi.llm.spend.refused{reason}` counts it.
  Cheap: with quotas off it is a cache hit; on, one monthly-cost read per LLM task. The circuit
  breaker ticket is not acquired for a refused call, and the rolling summary is not updated.
- **Dream (A2).** `DreamService` charges each consolidation call at the dream config's own prices
  (`pricedCost` — never the upper-bound estimate it uses for `maxCostPerRun`, so an unpriced config
  meters $0) and stops consolidating once the tenant budget is spent (not a failed cycle).
- **Caveats removed.** The WARN in `RestTenantQuota`, the "not enforced" notes in `TenantQuota`,
  `checkCostBudget` (which also wrongly said `AgentOrchestrator` called it per LLM turn) and
  `recordCost`, and the note in [`tenant-quotas.md`](../tenant-quotas.md), replaced with an
  accurate section listing the call classes that are **not** metered: rolling summary,
  tool-response summarization, group window/stance summaries, embeddings, the pre-pause segment of
  a tool-call HITL pause, lease-lost turns. The `MemberTurnExecutor` comment calling `AUDIT_COST` a
  running total is corrected (it is per step).
- **Docs/Operator.** `tenant-quotas.md`, `langchain.md` (prices drive the budget),
  `metrics.md`, `configuration-reference.md`; the Platform Operator prompt says the cap is enforced
  and priced-only (operator revision bumped; 20 at merge time).

**Tests:** `TurnCostMeterTest` (delta, pause→persist→resume once, prefix trap, outage retry,
exception swallowed, over-budget marked, in-memory end to end), `ConversationServiceCostMeteringTest`
(settle point through the real say path, unpriced, idempotent replay, lease loss),
`LlmTaskSpendGateTest`, `DreamServiceTest` additions, `ConversationServiceResumeTest` (resume
callback meters), `RestTenantQuotaTest` (no "NOT enforced" warning), `LlmTaskAuditLedgerTest` (cost now bypasses the collector gate),
`TenantCostMeteringIT` (Mongo + Postgres Testcontainers: two priced turns cross a $0.01 budget and
the gate then refuses; and a priced turn that pauses, is persisted to and reloaded from the real
conversation store, and resumes with further cost is charged exactly the step total, with
`audit:metered_cost` on the persisted step).

### Group cost

`GroupCostLedger` used to read only the member conversation's last step's `audit:cost`; main
fixed that independently (#1066, the snapshot now carries the conversation's summed cost). Tenant
metering does not go through it either way — member turns settle through `say`.

## 💰 feat(llm): per-conversation LLM cost ceiling `maxCostPerConversationUsd` (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/tenancy-cost-metering`)

### What changed and why

A new optional **agent-level** field, `maxCostPerConversationUsd` on
[`AgentConfiguration`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/agents/model/AgentConfiguration.java),
bounds the priced spend of one conversation — the sum of every step's `audit:cost`. Absent (every
existing agent) means unlimited and behaviour is unchanged; `0` allows no priced spend; a negative
value is unlimited. The task-level, tool-only `maxBudgetPerConversation` is untouched.

- **Carrier.** `AgentStoreClientLibrary` copies it onto the deployed `Agent`, which puts it on the
  memory in `startConversation` and in `continueConversation` (every say, rerun and HITL resume
  passes there, so the running version's value always wins and a version without one clears it).
  The memory carrier is transient and typed `OptionalDouble`, not a nullable `Double`: a mocked
  memory hands back `0.0` for a `Double`, which here means "no spend allowed".
- **Checks.** `LlmSpendGate` compares the conversation's spend with the ceiling before the tenant
  budget, once per LLM task before any model call (plain, streaming, tool loop, cascade); a refusal
  serves the fallback answer and records `llm:spend_refused:<taskId>` with
  `reason: conversation_ceiling`. Because that gate only sees a tool loop's cost when the loop ends,
  `ToolLoopRunner` repeats the check between rounds (prior spend + the loop's tokens at the task's
  prices + its tool cost) and ends the loop with a fixed answer saying the completed calls took
  effect. Counted on `eddi.llm.spend.refused{reason="conversation_ceiling"}`.
- **Docs.** [`langchain.md`](../langchain.md#per-conversation-llm-cost-ceiling) (new section),
  `tenant-quotas.md`, `metrics.md`.

**Tests:** `ConversationCostCeilingTest` (old JSON without the field, round trip, carrier set and
cleared, spend summed over steps without the metering marker, gate order and edge values),
`LlmTaskSpendGateTest` (ceiling refuses without asking the tenant budget; no ceiling sums nothing),
`ToolLoopRunnerCostCeilingTest` (stops at the ceiling, prior spend counts, no ceiling unchanged),
`AgentStoreClientLibraryMemoryToolsTest` (deployed agent carries the value).

### Not done

No Manager field: the agent editor has no cost/limits section and no nullable-number input, and
`turnDeadlineMs` — the nearest agent-level setting — is not editable there either. The value can be
set through the agent JSON. Follow-up: a "Limits" section for both.

## perf(groups): run the blocking group endpoints on virtual threads (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/group-endpoints-virtual-threads`

### What changed and why

This is the last part of the throughput plan's WS2 (item 5). Six group endpoints run discussion
work inline, and each one held a Quarkus worker thread for an entire multi-agent discussion,
every member turn and LLM call included:

- `discuss`
- `followUpWithMember`
- `continueDiscussion`
- `approveGroupPhase`
- `submitHumanInput`
- `approveGroupPhaseStreaming`, which calls `resumeDiscussion` on the request thread

The worker pool has about 200 threads and blocking REST endpoints share it, so a handful of
long discussions could starve unrelated requests. Conversation turns already left that pool
(PR #1045), and the member turns inside a discussion already run on virtual threads.

[`RestGroupConversation`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestGroupConversation.java)
now marks those six methods `@RunOnVirtualThread`. The other streaming endpoints were already
asynchronous and are unchanged: `discussStreaming` goes through `startAndDiscussAsync`, and
`continueDiscussionStreaming` uses its own executor.

### Design decisions

- **The annotation is on the implementing methods, not on `IRestGroupConversation`.** The JAX-RS
  contract stays free of execution-model detail. Whether Quarkus REST honours the annotation
  there was the open question; `GroupEndpointsVirtualThreadIT` settles it over HTTP.
- **The proof is an IT, not a unit test.** The execution model is chosen when Quarkus dispatches
  the request, so calling the bean directly cannot observe it. The IT installs a Mockito
  stand-in for `GroupConversationService` with `QuarkusMock` (Mockito, not a JDK proxy, because
  `QuarkusMock` needs an instance of the bean class). The stand-in records
  `Thread.isVirtual()` for each service call and completes the streaming listener.

### Verification

- `GroupEndpointsVirtualThreadIT`: 6 tests, one per endpoint. With every annotation removed,
  all 6 fail with "a worker thread".
- `GroupConversationIT` and `GroupHitlIT`, which drive real discussions, approvals and human
  input through these endpoints, pass (12 tests).
- 1,106 group unit tests pass, along with `ImportStyleTest`, `BuildQualityGatesTest` and
  `ChangelogRotationTest`.

## Outbound HTTP callers moved onto the facade; `SafeHttpClient` retired — consolidation phase 2 (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/outbound-http-callers` (stacked on `feat/outbound-http-facade`, labsai/EDDI#1042) · **Plan:** `planning/http-client-consolidation-plan.md`, Phase 2

**What changed**

Every EDDI-owned caller now sends through `OutboundHttp`, with its existing caps, timeouts and redirect rules unchanged:

| Caller | SSRF / redirects | Body |
|---|---|---|
| `WeatherTool`, `WebSearchTool` | `HOPS_ONLY` (fixed hosts), JDK-safe redirects | `bytes(1 MiB / 2 MiB, TRUNCATE)`; a truncated answer is refused as before |
| `PdfReaderTool`, `WebScraperTool` | the tool's own URL check, then `HOPS_ONLY` | `bytes(eddi.tools.*, TRUNCATE)` |
| `AttachmentForwarder` | `STRICT` | `bytes(per-file cap, TRUNCATE)` |
| `SafeHttpPageFetcher` (crawler) | `STRICT` | `bytes(cap, TRUNCATE)`; its hand-written bounded read and watchdog are gone (identical in the facade), and so is its `IRuntime` dependency |
| `OAuthTokenClient` | `NONE` (the credential-endpoint allowlist is the gate), `NEVER` | `string(1 MiB, FAIL)`. **New cap**: a token answer was unbounded before; anything over 1 MiB is now a transient `TOKEN_ENDPOINT_UNAVAILABLE` |
| `SlackWebApiClient` | `NONE` (fixed host), `NEVER` | `string(1 MiB, FAIL)`; its private `HttpClient` is gone. **Now bounded in time** — it had no request timeout at all, and gets the facade's 15 s per hop |
| `A2AToolProviderManager` | `STRICT` or `METADATA_ONLY` per `eddi.security.ssrf-protection.enabled`, `NEVER` | `bytes(1 MiB, TRUNCATE)`; its lazy private client and its `a2a-body-watchdog` thread are gone |
| `OpenApiSpecFetcher` (MCP/httpcall OpenAPI discovery, added on `main` while this PR was open) | its own redirect loop with `OutboundTargetPolicy` on every hop, each hop one `NEVER` send with `SsrfPolicy.NONE` (the policy is the gate) | `stream(16 MiB, 4 × 30 s)`; its private `openapi-spec-body-watchdog` thread is gone. Its "did not arrive within" message now names the real budget (120 s), not the request timeout |
| `RemoteApiResourceSource` (+ the two static helpers) | new profile `INTERNAL_PEER`: `NONE` (`SourceUrlValidator` stays the gate), `NEVER` | JSON `string(eddi.backup.sync.max-response-bytes, FAIL)` with the old refusal message; archives `bytes(256 MiB, FAIL)` |

- **`SafeHttpClient` is deleted** (internal API, no callers left), with `CappedStringBodyHandler` and the per-source client lifecycle (`close()`, `configure()`, the owned-client flag): sources share the application's facade. `BoundedBodyReader` moved into `engine/httpclient/outbound` as package-private.
- `RestImportService` gets the facade by setter injection, lazily falling back to a standalone one outside CDI, as `A2AToolProviderManager` does.
- AGENTS.md (§4.2 table, §4.4 Tool Security and its sample, Key Files), README, `architecture.md` (SSRF layers and sample), `connections.md`, `code-review-standards.md`, `security-review.md`, `a2a-protocol.md`, `configuration-reference.md` and `metrics.md` now name `OutboundHttp`.
- The `// codeql[java/ssrf]` suppressions moved with the sinks: they now sit on the `http.send(...)` calls in `RemoteApiResourceSource` (and its `sendJson` helper).

**Tests**

- The 22 server-backed `SafeHttpClient` tests were ported to `OutboundHttpServerTest` against the facade on the real JDK engine (a no-op `hopValidator` replaces the Mockito spy that let a redirect reach loopback). Their subjects, and a new cross-origin `Authorization` case, are all still covered over real sockets.
- New test helpers in `engine/httpclient/outbound` (test sources): `TestOutbound` (standalone facade, facade over any engine, canned responses), `ScriptedEngine` (records hops, replays answers), and `JdkClientEngine` (routes facade hops into a mocked JDK `HttpClient`). The last one let roughly 200 remote-sync test stubs stay as they were while the code under test runs through the real facade.
- The crawler, OAuth, A2A wiring and new Slack tests run the real facade over a scripted engine rather than mocking it. New: a loopback crawl seed is refused before anything is sent; an A2A peer's 307 and a Slack 302 are never followed; a sync source declaring a body over `eddi.backup.sync.max-response-bytes` is refused before it is read (the old size tests only simulated the refusal in a stub, so lifting the cap broke none of them).
- Mutation-checked: dropping `NEVER` from OAuth, A2A, Slack or remote sync, weakening the crawler to `HOPS_ONLY`, or lifting the remote-sync size cap each fails a test.

**Design decisions**

- `internal-peer` has its own connection pool but no configuration keys yet. Remote sync had nothing tunable, so it uses the `default` settings, and the configuration reference says so.
- Pass-through, not rewrite: every caller keeps its old checks (the tools' own `validateUrl`, A2A's pre-credential check, `SourceUrlValidator`). The facade's policy is added on top.

**Next:** Phase 3, the langchain4j adapter: shared LLM provider pools, plus `llm-provider` keys.

## Outbound HTTP facade — Phase 1 of the client consolidation (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/outbound-http-facade` · **Plan:** `planning/http-client-consolidation-plan.md`, Phase 1

**What changed**

- New package `ai.labs.eddi.engine.httpclient.outbound`. `OutboundHttp` is the one place outbound policy lives: the SSRF policy per request (`STRICT`, `HOPS_ONLY`, `METADATA_ONLY`, `NONE`, `FOLLOW_GLOBAL_FLAG`); redirects followed hop by hop with a `MethodRule` (`JDK_SAFE`, the RFC 9110 rule `SafeHttpClient` always used, and `VERTX_COMPAT`, which matches what the `httpcalls` Vert.x client does); cross-origin stripping of the fixed `SENSITIVE_HEADERS` **plus any header the request marks caller-derived** (a `${caller:token}` can sit in any header name); one whole-exchange deadline; redirect bodies dropped after 64 KiB; body caps enforced while reading (`BodyMode.string/bytes/file/stream/discard`, `OnOverflow.TRUNCATE|FAIL`); refusal on a Vert.x event-loop thread; four meters (`eddi.http.outbound.requests|duration|blocked|bytes`) on a new **Outbound HTTP** dashboard row.
- `OutboundEngine` is a one-hop SPI; `JdkOutboundEngine` keeps one `java.net.http` client per `Profile` (`default`, `llm-provider`, `apicalls`, `internal-peer`), with an optional forward proxy (vault-resolvable credentials, answered to proxy challenges only, failing closed when unresolved) and an optional named `quarkus.tls` configuration.
- `SafeHttpClient` is now a `@Deprecated` adapter over the facade. Its API and behaviour are unchanged: all 22 server-backed `SafeHttpClientTest`/`SafeHttpClientBodyBoundsTest` cases pass as they were, apart from one import (`CallerFrames` moved). Its unit tests of moved internals were ported: the method rule to `MethodRuleTest`, the budget and redirect-body discard to `OutboundHttpTest`, and the `withDefaultTimeout` rebuild to a request-conversion test.
- New settings, documented in [configuration-reference.md](../configuration-reference.md#outbound-http-client): `eddi.http.outbound.engine`, `eddi.http.outbound.default.{connect-timeout,total-timeout,protocol,tls.configuration-name}`, `eddi.http.outbound.proxy.{host,port,non-proxy-hosts,credentials}`. The connect timeout falls back to `httpClient.connectTimeoutInMillis`, so a tuned deployment keeps its value.

**Design decisions**

- Only the `default` profile has keys. The others get theirs in the phase that moves their callers, so no documented setting is a silent no-op in the meantime.
- **`tls.named-groups` was dropped from this phase.** `java.net.http.HttpClient` copies the SSL parameters it is given and discards `namedGroups`: `client.sslParameters().getNamedGroups()` comes back `null`. A key would therefore be a no-op. PQC plan PQ-3 needs another route: an `SSLContext` wrapper that sets groups per engine, the global `jdk.tls.namedGroups`, or the Vert.x engine.
- `max-connections-per-host` was deferred to the Vert.x engine (Phase 4), because the JDK client has no per-host pool limit.
- `OutboundBlockedException` extends `IllegalArgumentException` and `OutboundTimeoutException` extends `HttpTimeoutException`, so every existing `catch` keeps working.
- The plan's table maps `send*` to `NONE`, but what `send` actually did was trust the initial URL and fully validate every redirect hop. That is `SsrfPolicy.HOPS_ONLY`, a policy the plan did not list.
- The event-loop guard was enabled after an audit: the only `Uni`/`@NonBlocking` code in `src/main/java` is `NamelessPrincipalAugmentor`, which makes no outbound call.
- Mutation-checked: removing caller-derived stripping, the `VERTX_COMPAT` RETURN, the redirect-hop check, the FAIL-mode cap probe and the redirect-body cap each fails a test.

**Next:** Phase 2 moves the callers (`OAuthTokenClient`, `SafeHttpPageFetcher`, `AttachmentForwarder`, the tools, `SlackWebApiClient`, `A2AToolProviderManager`, `RemoteApiResourceSource`) onto `OutboundHttp` and retires `SafeHttpClient`.

## docs(security): cryptography inventory and post-quantum readiness, guarded by a test (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/pqc-crypto-inventory`

First step (PQ-1) of the post-quantum readiness work. It changes no runtime behaviour.

### What changed and why

- **New page [`docs/cryptography.md`](../cryptography.md)** lists every algorithm EDDI uses (vault AES-256-GCM and PBKDF2, the HMAC-SHA256 audit ledger and vault checksums, Ed25519 agent signing, SHA-256 hashing, and TLS), what each one protects, and whether it is post-quantum safe. It also covers the threat model (harvest-now-decrypt-later for TLS, future forgery of Ed25519 signatures) and the roadmap. The page is linked from [`security.md`](../security.md) (TLS section and See Also) and from `SUMMARY.md`.
- **Verified on JDK 25.0.1:** JSSE's default named groups are classical only (`x25519, secp256r1, secp384r1, secp521r1, x448, ffdhe*`), while `ML-DSA-65` and `ML-KEM-768` are available through the standard JCA (an ML-DSA-65 signature is 3309 B and its public key 1974 B). The page states only these verified facts about TLS.
- **New `CryptoInventoryTest`** scans `src/main/java` for the JCA engine `getInstance(...)` calls and `new SecretKeySpec(...)`. It resolves each algorithm from a literal or a same-file `static final String`, and fails the build in four cases:
  - the algorithm is not on its allowlist;
  - the argument cannot be resolved, so moving the string into a variable cannot bypass the check;
  - the call pins a JCA provider;
  - an allowlist entry is no longer used, or is not named in `docs/cryptography.md`.

### Design decisions

- Rather than scan only the per-class constants listed in the plan, the test resolves each call's argument, so a new call site cannot slip through by using an unfamiliar constant name.
- `TrustManagerFactory`, `KeyManagerFactory` and `KeyStore` are left out of the scan: they name PKIX and trust-store formats rather than primitives.
- The allowlist is two-way (unused entries fail as well) so the doc cannot drift from the code.

### Next

PQ-5a: make the existing agent signatures on audit entries versioned and verifiable (see `planning/pqc-readiness-plan.md`).

---

## ✨ feat(rag): live run progress, a knowledge-base inspector and schedule status for ingestion (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/rag-ingestion-ux`)

### What changed and why

Ingestion ran blind: a run row was written as `RUNNING` with zeroes and only got its numbers when it
ended, nothing said what a knowledge base actually held, and a source's schedule was a cron string
with no word on whether it was armed, firing or failing. The backend now answers all three.

**Run visibility**

- `IIngestionStateStore.reportProgress(run)` writes a running run's counters onto its row, fenced on
  `status = RUNNING` in both stores, so a late write can neither reopen nor overwrite a finished or
  reaped run. `IngestionPipeline`'s collector calls it at most every two seconds (forced once at the
  start, so the trigger is on the row before the first document); a failed write is a stale number,
  never a failed run.
- `IngestionRun` gains `documentsSkipped`, `stopReason` and `trigger` (`MANUAL` / `SCHEDULED`), with a
  13-argument constructor kept for the existing callers. Postgres gains the columns through
  `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`; Mongo needs nothing. The skipped count and stop reason were already in
  `IngestionReport` and reached only the preview response and the log.
- `RagSourceIngestionService.runAsync` takes the trigger; `processScheduledFire` passes `SCHEDULED`.
  A run cancelled at shutdown is now closed with the counters its last progress write recorded rather
  than zeroes.
- `POST …/sources/{sourceId}/run` returns the `runId` it reserved.

**Inspector** — `KnowledgeBaseInspector`, read-only:

- `GET …/sources/{sourceId}/documents` (VIEW) — the source's document states, most recently embedded
  first, filterable and paged, labelled by file name for uploads; the orphan-sweep marker row is hidden.
- `GET …/sources/{sourceId}/chunks?documentId=` (EDIT) — one document's chunks in document order.
- `POST /ragstore/rags/{id}/search` (EDIT) — a test retrieval with the knowledge base's own
  `maxResults`/`minScore` as defaults, optionally narrowed to one source.
- The vector stores have no listing, so chunks are reached by a similarity search filtered on the
  metadata ingestion writes, and always ANDed with `EmbeddingStoreFactory.retrievalFilter` — the
  inspector sees exactly what retrieval would, and a per-id knowledge base in a shared table sees only
  its own chunks. A store that cannot filter answers 501; a failing provider or store 502 with the cause.

**Schedules**

- `IScheduleStore.readScheduleByName` (Mongo + Postgres), and `GET …/sources/{sourceId}/schedule`
  (VIEW) returning next/last fire, fire status, fail count, disable reason and the last five fires.
- `GET /ragstore/rags/cron/preview` applies the save-time checks — parseable, matches a real date, not
  more frequent than `eddi.schedule.min-interval-seconds` — and returns the next fire times in UTC.

### Design decisions

- **Chunks and search need EDIT, the document list VIEW.** A published knowledge base grants VIEW to
  everyone; the chunk text is the knowledge base's content and every call costs an embedding.
- **Progress is throttled in the pipeline, not in the store.** One small update per source every two
  seconds; the interval is an instance field only so a test can make every document report.
- **Search goes through the retrieval isolation filter unconditionally.** An inspector that could see
  another knowledge base's chunks in a shared table would be a cross-tenant read.

### Tests

`IngestionStateStoreContract` (three new cases, run against Mongo, Postgres and the in-memory double),
`IngestionPipelineTest` (trigger + stop reason recorded; counters visible on the running row while it
embeds), `RagSourceIngestionServiceTest` (shutdown keeps progress), `KnowledgeBaseInspectorTest`
(real `InMemoryEmbeddingStore`: ordering, isolation, source narrowing, min score, file labels),
`RestRagIngestionInspectionTest` (access levels, clamping, 400/404/501/502), `RagSourceScheduleViewTest`,
and `readScheduleByName` in both schedule store container tests.

**Files:** [`IIngestionStateStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/IIngestionStateStore.java),
[`IngestionPipeline.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/IngestionPipeline.java),
[`KnowledgeBaseInspector.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/KnowledgeBaseInspector.java),
[`RagSourceIngestionService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/RagSourceIngestionService.java),
[`IRestRagIngestion.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/IRestRagIngestion.java),
[`RestRagIngestion.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/rest/RestRagIngestion.java),
[`IScheduleStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/schedule/IScheduleStore.java), both state
and schedule stores, [`docs/rag.md`](../rag.md).

### What's next

The cost controls planned in [`rag-ingestion-source-types-plan.md`](../../planning/rag-ingestion-source-types-plan.md)
(§3.13) and run outcome/duration metrics. A cancel button for a running run would need a
cancellation signal the worker polls; `isCancelled` is the natural hook.

## ✨ feat(manager): see ingestion while and after it runs — progress, run details, schedule, contents, test retrieval (2026-10-15)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/rag-ingestion-ux`)

### What changed and why

The knowledge-base editor showed a spinner during a run, "N ingested · M unchanged" after it, a bare
cron string for the schedule, and nothing at all about what the knowledge base held. It now uses the
endpoints added in the backend entry of the same date.

- **Live progress** (`ingestion-run-history.tsx` → `RunProgress`): documents looked at against what the
  last complete run saw (or the file count, for a Files source), every counter as it moves, the trigger,
  and elapsed time against the source's time budget. The run history polls every 2 s while a run is in
  flight (was 3 s).
- **Run history** opens each run to all it recorded — skipped, failed, removed, chunks, duration,
  estimated cost and how it ended, the stop reason said in words. A completed run that lost documents
  gets an amber icon instead of the green tick, and its summary says how many failed.
- **Errors say what the server said.** Run, Preview and Purge failures append the server's message to
  the generic line ("…A run may already be in flight." was shown for a disabled source and for a
  database fault alike).
- **Schedule** (`ingestion-schedule.tsx`): a preset picker (hand only, hourly, daily, weekly, monthly —
  UTC) or a custom cron checked against the server's rules while it is typed, with its next fire times
  in local time. Once saved, `ScheduleStatus` shows the next run, the last fire, recent fires with their
  errors, and says in words when a saved cron produced no schedule, when fires are failing, when the
  scheduler disabled it and when it was dead-lettered. The card header shows the preset's name with the
  raw cron on hover.
- **Ingested documents** (`ingestion-source-contents.tsx`): the source's documents, filterable and
  paged, each Live, Missed by the last N runs or Removed; opening one shows its chunks. Collapsed by
  default — each opened document is an embedding call on the server, so chunks are fetched only on
  demand, never retried, and kept for five minutes.
- **Test Retrieval** (`retrieval-tester.tsx`): a new editor section that searches the whole knowledge
  base or one source exactly as an agent would and shows each chunk with its score and origin.
- After a run ends the document list and schedule refetch (`useRefreshAfterRun`), keyed on the newest
  run's id and status rather than a timer.
- The legacy **Document Ingestion** section now says that pasted documents cannot be listed, updated
  or removed later, and points to a Files source.
- **Schedules page:** an ingestion schedule links to the knowledge base it is changed on.

### Design decisions

- **No `count` interpolation** in the new strings, so none of them needs per-locale plural categories;
  the numbers read naturally with neutral wording ("Missed by the last {{runs}} run(s)").
- **The source card observes the files query only while a run is in flight**, for the progress total.
  Observing it always refetched the list every time a card was opened — caught by the existing
  "reloads the file list" test.
- The run id `POST …/run` now returns is not used: `ApiClient` discards every 202 body, and the
  `RUNNING` row in the history identifies the run.

### Tests

`resource-detail-rag-insight.test.tsx` (9 cases: progress estimate and bar, run details and stop
reason, server error text, schedule status / missing schedule, live cron check, documents → filter →
chunks, retrieval request and result, retrieval failure), one case in `schedules-regressions.test.tsx`;
the cron assertion in `resource-detail-rag-sources.test.tsx` now checks the preset label and the raw
cron in its title. 87 new keys in all 11 locales; `npm run i18n:check`, `lint` and `typecheck` pass.
The five new operations are added to `openapi-operations.json` (refreshed from a Maven-built spec), which the MSW contract test checks every handler against. Checked in the browser against the mock API.
